# Round 7 of the relation-map review. Previous: audit_evidence_claims/m1, m2b, m3, m4, m5, m6 REPORT.md. Repairs: CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md (all rounds), latest commit on HEAD. Tests: 331.
Design now: link_entities = (1) the intervention resolver (normalize.resolve_drug_string) runs on each list piece; a drug found inside a piece it refuses as a distinct product/tracer is never linked, unless the same molecule is accepted in another piece; (2) an input that is exactly one stored name, not refused, is read whole; (3) otherwise the list-item linker (_link): items cut at , ; (ASCII/CJK), / and | anywhere, and listing words outside parentheses; an item with any qualifier refuses all its drugs; a bare qualifier carries over a listing word; names never span items.
1. Re-run every earlier reproduction (m1 to m6); FIXED / NOT FIXED per finding. Reuse earlier scripts.
2. Attack for originator leaks: inputs where BOTH the resolver and the list-item linker miss a refusal; and for demo regressions and cost. Over-refusal is intended fail-closed behaviour unless it drops a valid drug that is alone in its item or a demo. Known over-refusal: M5-S2.
Same approval bar as below. Write scratch files only under audit_evidence_claims/m7/, report in audit_evidence_claims/m7/REPORT.md. Do not edit tracked files.
End with exactly one line: VERDICT: APPROVE or VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED.

--- original brief ---
# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent relation-map review — round 6

Reviewed HEAD **`b7a00f9`**, including repairs `595f5f9` and `c43f911`; baseline feature commit `bfcb83d`. Date: 2026-11-03 KST. **Three reproducible originator-leak mechanisms block approval.** All exact named m1–m4 blockers remain fixed. M5's reported spaced-separator examples are fixed, but its earlier internal-separator attack still fails. M5-B2 and M5-S1 are fixed for their reported inputs; M5-S3's reproduced cost defect is fixed. M5-S2 remains the explicitly accepted over-refusal.

No application or tracked file was edited. The intentional refusal of all drugs in one qualified item is accepted throughout this report. A product's originator being accepted is a different outcome and is not covered by that exception.

## Verification and evidence boundaries

- Required command: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` — **329 passed in 30.72 s**, exit 0. Bytecode disabled; all test temporary files confined to m6. [Output](root_checks/pytest.txt), [environment and hashes](root_checks/pytest_summary.json).
- Original CLI server `python -B -u src/app.py <port>` exercised over HTTP on owned ports **8896, 8897 and 8899**, sequentially when reusing a port. No use of 8765. Six inherited HTTP runners exited 0; all owned servers were closed. [Replay execution](root_checks/execution_http.json), [copy provenance](root_checks/reuse_manifest.json).
- **6,469 live centres**, **22 relation/direction/type/role combinations**, **0 stored-row oracle mismatches**. **19/19 DB assertions** and **24/24 UI assertions** pass. [DB/UI report and exact fixtures](db_ui/REPORT.md), [oracle](db_ui/db_validate.json), [DB assertions](db_ui/db_assertions.json), [UI assertions](db_ui/ui_assertions.json).
- **36/36 complete page-demo payloads equal m5**; all 36 demo callbacks plus 6 map callbacks succeed. [Comparison](db_ui/ui_demo_compare.json), [callbacks](db_ui/ui_m3_checks.json). No demo regression was found.
- Thirteen inherited name scripts were reused with output plumbing adapted, including m1 reproductions, m2b/m3 boundaries, m4 attacks, and the m5 **113 boundary cases / 7,511 unique alias-key sweep**. [Execution](names/replay_execution.json), [provenance](names/replay_manifest.json), [name disposition report](names/REPORT.md). Fresh independent attacks add **106 inputs / 8 QA responses**, plus **11 reversed-qualifier cases**. [Fresh results](attacks/fresh_results.json), [reversed cases](attacks/reversed_results.json).
- Page JavaScript was executed in a Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch stubs. This establishes generated markup, callbacks, error handling and request ordering; it does **not** establish browser paint/layout or browser-executed exploit behavior. Actual served HTML equals the worktree. No browser-executed XSS claim is made.
- Root-owned HTTP/test/cost evidence is under `root_checks/`; worker evidence is under `names/`, `db_ui/`, and `attacks/`. Unattributed files that appeared directly in m6 during the review are not used as evidence. The final integrity check compares against the root review's own initial tracked-file hashes.

## Blocking findings

### M6-B1 — P1: a separator glued on both sides still hides a refused suffix

Exact request:

```json
{"text":"bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib"}
```

`POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 with **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, and **no refused span**. The identical `/api/resolve` input returns `bevacizumab - awwb: BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no accepted first drug, and `osimertinib: EXACT / CHEMBL3353410`. Thus the refused-product premise is demonstrated using the repository's own resolver. [Actual HTTP](root_checks/confirm_http.json), [independent replay](db_ui/interior_separator_probe.json).

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Actual result |
|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib?` | 12 originator rows; first `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib?` | 12 originator rows; first `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

**Cause:** `src/query.py:143-154` splits comma/semicolon and whitespace, strips `/` and `|` only from the edges of a token, and creates boundaries only for `startswith`/`endswith`. Interior `awwb/osimertinib` remains one token. `_qualified` (`99-110`) never checks a run ending at `awwb`, so the anchored suffix guard (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213-223`) misses it. The separate bare `bevacizumab` token is then accepted at `src/query.py:212-237`, and `Engine.explore` trusts it at `src/app.py:88-95`.

`bevacizumab - awwb|osimertinib` also leaks. The pipe resolver itself misses that split, so the slash case is the strongest exact resolver comparison. Controls `bevacizumab - awwb/ osimertinib` and `bevacizumab - awwb / osimertinib` correctly select only OSIMERTINIB and disclose the refused product; bare `bevacizumab - awwb` refuses. These controls isolate the missing **interior** boundary.

Related consequences of the same mechanism are `Erlotinib/hydrochloride` and `Erlotinib|hydrochloride` assembling **CHEMBL1079742**, a salt across a declared item boundary; `biosimilar anastrozole/Arimidex` losing the separate Arimidex; and `osimertinib/EGFR` returning not_found. [HTTP](root_checks/alias_http.json). They are not counted as separate blockers.

**Status:** inherited under the exact `514929c` linker comparison; the slash input was already in m5's reused attack. M5-B1 is therefore **FIXED for the reported spaced examples, NOT FIXED as a mechanism**. [Previous/current comparison](attacks/fresh_results.json), [m5 replay](names/names_m5_attack.json).

**Required outcome:** all declared glued/free separators must form real item boundaries before guards and matching. Never select CHEMBL1201583 from this refused component; preserving the separately named osimertinib or refusing an ambiguous input is safe.

### M6-B2 — P1: edge punctuation is removed for alias matching after defeating refusal

Exact input **`bevacizumab-awwb。`**, with final **U+3002 IDEOGRAPHIC FULL STOP**, returns HTTP 200 and **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, matched alias `Bevacizumab awwb`, and no refusal. The same `/api/resolve` input yields a single `bevacizumab-awwb: BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` component with no accepted drug. [HTTP](root_checks/confirm_http.json).

`Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb。?` and `Which companies sponsor bevacizumab-awwb。?` each return **12 originator evidence rows**, with the same first trial/sponsor edges as B1. ASCII-period `bevacizumab-awwb.` and bare `bevacizumab-awwb` are refused; their trial/sponsor questions have **0 evidence**. Trailing U+2014, trailing ASCII hyphen and leading ASCII hyphen also produce the resolver/linker disagreement over HTTP.

**Cause:** the strip set at `src/query.py:147` differs from the resolver's edge-punctuation normalization (`src/textrules.py:283,382-383`). The suffix regex is anchored at both ends (`156-157`), so the retained punctuation defeats it. The later `canonical_key` call (`src/query.py:221`; `src/textrules.py:339-346`) removes that punctuation and recovers an alias for the originator. Whole-item and eight-word checks preserve the same offending character, so increasing their coverage cannot fix this mismatch.

**Status:** inherited under `514929c`, still a blocker. Quoted/bracketed suffix variants and some Unicode hyphens also fail both resolver and linker; those are explicitly distinguished from the exact U+3002 resolver disagreement in [independent findings](attacks/FINDINGS.md). No external pharmacology assumption is needed for the primary reproduction.

**Required outcome:** normalization that enables a positive alias must not remove evidence that prevented its refusal guard from matching. The U+3002 and hyphen-edge controls must refuse the product before mapping or retrieving originator evidence.

### M6-B3 — P1: forward-only qualifier carry loses a trailing qualifier inside one product description

Two exact parenthetical descriptions independently fail:

| Exact input | `/api/resolve` | `/api/explore` |
|---|---|---|
| `doxorubicin (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)` | One `doxorubicin` component, `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no accepted drug; rules `extract_parenthetical`, `distinct_product_guard` | DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463, 3 trials, no refusal |
| `bevacizumab (investigational and biosimilar)` | One `bevacizumab` component, `BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT`, no accepted drug; same rules | BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583, 122 trials, no refusal |

These are qualified descriptions of the preceding drug, not two separately named drugs. [Actual HTTP](root_checks/carry_http.json), [independent current/predecessor comparison](attacks/reversed_results.json).

`Which trials evaluate doxorubicin (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)?` returns **3** originator rows, first `NCT01970540|EVALUATES|CHEMBL53463|NCT01970540`. Its sponsor question returns 3 rows, first `ORG:imunon|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL53463|`. The corresponding two bevacizumab questions return **12** originator rows each, beginning with B1's trial/sponsor edges.

**Cause:** `src/query.py:167-174` splits on `and` even inside the parenthetical qualifier. The drug is in the earlier, unqualified item. The later `liposomal formulation)` or `biosimilar)` item has no drug, so `192-197` stores it as `dangling` and continues. Carry is applied only to a **subsequent** drug (`183-184,199-201`); it never marks the earlier drug refused before final positive matching. The final qualifier can therefore disappear without even an output Refused entry. The full resolver preserves the original product context for its guard (`src/normalize.py:707,729-735`).

Controls `doxorubicin (liposomal formulation)` and the original M5-B2 `liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin` both refuse and produce **0 QA evidence**. Separate-drug controls `osimertinib and liposomal doxorubicin` and `doxorubicin and liposomal paclitaxel` retain the independently named first drug. [HTTP controls](root_checks/carry_http.json).

**Status:** inherited under `514929c`; the latest forward-carry repair does not solve this design failure. The stated carry direction explains the implementation but does not waive the approval bar against a refused product becoming its originator. This is an **under-refusal**, not the accepted same-item over-refusal or M5-S2.

**Required outcome:** preserve the qualifier's scope within an unambiguously single product description, or refuse that ambiguous product as a whole. Do not propagate every trailing qualifier backwards indiscriminately across genuinely separate named products.

## Should-fix and design consequences, separate from blockers

### M6-S1 — P2: complete stored names containing list punctuation can select a different drug

The strict item policy can do more than refuse a lone alias: six complete stored aliases in the reused 7,511-key sweep now select only another drug ID. Examples confirmed over actual HTTP:

| Complete stored name / alias | Stored unique identity | Current selected identity |
|---|---|---|
| `ENDOSTATIN, N-TERMINAL-MGGSHHHHH` — preferred name | CHEMBL4297946 | ENDOSTATIN / CHEMBL4297747 |
| `Rapamycin, 42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-` | EVEROLIMUS / CHEMBL1908360 | SIROLIMUS / CHEMBL413 |
| `CP-358,774` | ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553 | ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742 |

[HTTP and QA](root_checks/alias_http.json), [all six aliases, stored provenance and previous/current hits](names/names_m5_attack.json), [summary table](names/REPORT.md). The other examples are `Usp mab 002, monoclonal igg1`, `Usp mab 003, monoclonal igg1`, and `12,13-desoxyepothilone b, cis`. The endostatin exact-ID control selects the intended CHEMBL4297946. The complete endostatin name's trial QA instead retrieves two CHEMBL4297747 rows.

This follows the unconditional item boundaries at `src/query.py:143,216-217,277`. Because the requested design explicitly imposes those boundaries, this is reported as an identity tradeoff/should-fix, not used as an additional approval blocker or mislabeled as an over-refusal. It needs an explicit user-visible limitation or a safe disambiguation policy. Do not bypass product guards merely because a full alias exists.

The same sweep records **57** formerly recognized lone aliases with no current Drug hit against the exact bf436c6 comparison: the accepted pegylated alias plus **56** punctuation/list-word cases such as `ICI 182,780`, `CP-675,206`, `Nk cells, autologous`, and `Anusol plus hc`. These deliberate boundary losses are recorded without treating intended over-refusal as a new blocker. None appears in the page demos. [summary](names/names_m5_summary.json).

Previously recorded lowercase-ID/name handling, blank submit, raw 400 details and Infinity behavior remain as itemized above. No new misleading cut notice, graph relation/direction, stored field value, or generated-markup injection defect was established in the exercised paths.

## Cost, demo and boundary controls

The exact m5 performance script was reused; its `m4` baseline means bf436c6, not the slower m5 revision. Fresh instrumented HEAD uses **2,223 calls to each refusal predicate / 32,882 aggregate characters** for the 250-token plain case; the prior m5 report recorded 31,625 calls each / 10,479,750 characters. The bounded eight-word scan removes the former all-substring growth. [instrumentation](root_checks/perf_results.json).

| Exact fresh HTTP workload | Observed result |
|---|---|
| `/api/explore`, `" ".join(["x"] * 250)` — 499 chars | 200, **0.0302 s** |
| `/api/ask`, same 499 chars | 200, **0.0308 s** |
| `/api/explore`, 244 x tokens plus osimertinib — 499 chars | 200, **0.0796 s** |
| Four simultaneous 499-character explores | All 200, **0.1290 s** total wall time |
| Ordinary EGFR sent 0.1 s after one long lookup | 200, **0.0385 s**; not proof it waited on an occupied lock |
| 501-character explore | 400, **0.0030 s** |
| `/api/check`, question EGFR, 999-character x answer | 200, **0.1812 s**; following EGFR 200, **0.0402 s** |
| `/api/check`, question EGFR, 3,999-character x answer | 200, **0.3930 s** |

[HTTP timing/lock replay](root_checks/perf_http.json), [previous answer-timeout reproduction](root_checks/perf_check_http.json), [larger answer-field HTTP](root_checks/confirm_http.json). These are local observations, not universal latency guarantees; first replay workloads overlapped with other read-only audit work.

An additional isolated three-sample direct-linker check gives medians **0.0143, 0.0277, 0.0569, 0.1139 s** at 250/500/1,000/2,000 plain tokens. A 3,999-character semicolon-separated answer takes **0.1395 s** to link / **0.3340 s** for `Engine.check`; many matched drugs, many refused items and many joiners also complete below those check timings. [Exact inputs and measurements](root_checks/cost_stress.json), [reproducer](cost_stress.py). The overall linker still has per-segment rescans and growing set operations (`src/query.py:180-186,214`); this report does not infer global linear complexity from `_qualified` alone. No further practical cost blocker was demonstrated within the HTTP caps.

All **16** new padded suffix/tracer/liposomal/generic-version controls with 0/4/8/16 filler words refuse. No isolated eight-word-window regression was found. Forward qualifier-carry and standalone product controls (`Mvasi and bevacizumab`, bracketed/trademark Mvasi, `Abraxane and paclitaxel`) behave as specified. [fresh attack controls](attacks/fresh_results.json).

All seven actual HTTP QA demos retain evidence counts **12, 12, 11, 1, 12, 0, 0**. Six full responses equal bfcb83d; Mvasi differs only in refusal metadata. All 36 complete demo responses equal m5. [HTTP demo comparison](root_checks/http_demo_results.json), [full demo comparison](db_ui/ui_demo_compare.json).

Ordinary Disease HTTP returns 2,774 Trials and 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 80.714 ms**. The original **24 requests / 12 workers** all return expected HTTP 200 shapes in **753.288 ms**. A 1,001-Drug fixture verifies all three count chunks. [HTTP](root_checks/http_results.json), [fixtures](db_ui/db_fixtures.json).

VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED
# Independent relation-map review — round 5

Reviewed HEAD: `514929c83fb7ab8cdd23fdd50eb300bd409c3250`, 2026-11-03. Immediate predecessor: `bf436c6`; cumulative repair comparison: `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD`. Specification: the user's round-5 brief, original approval bar, [m1](../m1/REPORT.md), [m2b](../m2b/REPORT.md), [m3](../m3/REPORT.md), [m4](../m4/REPORT.md), and `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`.

**Approval remains blocked by two independently reproduced product-refusal bypasses.** An attached separator defeats a suffix refusal; a listing word can detach a formulation qualifier from its sole drug. Both draw the refused product's originator and return that originator's QA evidence. Exact predecessor comparisons show both are inherited bypasses, newly identified here, rather than newly introduced by this commit. All previously named blockers are FIXED for their exact inputs under the revised fail-closed policy.

The new implementation also merges drug aliases across its own comma/semicolon boundaries. This can select the wrong salt or suppress a valid drug alone in another item. Those findings, a lone-alias rejection, and input cost are separated from the decisive product-refusal blockers below. Ordinary over-refusal of drugs sharing one item is **accepted behavior**, not a finding.

| Bare `erlotinib hydrochloride`; `Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid` | **FIXED / retained** | Correct complete alias identity; no nested parent relink; separately named parent still links. `src/query.py:174-198`; [controls](names_attack.json). |
| Supplementary `osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab` | **FIXED under revised design** | Both same-item drugs refused. The old goal of retaining osimertinib here is superseded; not a should-fix. [HTTP](attack_http.json). |
| Supplementary `generic version of Paclitaxel conjugate with poly-l-glutamic acid` | **FIXED** | Refusal, no centre or qualified-product QA evidence. [HTTP](attack_http.json). |
| Supplementary `paclitaxel with albumin-bound nanoparticles` | **FIXED** | Hyphenated and unhyphenated forms refuse. [Replay](names_new_boundaries.json). |
| Supplementary semicolon/fullwidth comma: `osimertinib; generic version of bevacizumab`; `osimertinib， generic version of bevacizumab` | **FIXED** | Separate osimertinib survives; bevacizumab refused. [Separator replay](names_separator_checks.json). |
| Lowercase IDs / Investigator names / raw request-shape details | **NOT FIXED — unchanged** | Same m1 qualifications. |
| Empty/whitespace map form | **NOT FIXED — unchanged usability qualification** | No request; previous view remains. `web/try.html:241`; [UI](ui_m3_checks.json). |
| Positive/negative Infinity fixture | **NOT FIXED — unchanged, failure closed** | `_number` accepts Infinity; JSON encoding returns HTTP 400; no invalid number drawn. None in snapshot. `src/app.py:207-208,264-269`; [fixture](db_numeric_fixture.json). |

## Blocking findings

### M5-B1 — P1: attached separator hides a known refused suffix

Exact unchanged-server request:

```json
{"text":"bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib"}
```

`POST /api/explore` returns HTTP 200 with **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, OSIMERTINIB only in `also`, and **no refusal**. The linker reports `matched_text:'bevacizumab-awwb/'`, `matched_alias:'Bevacizumab awwb'`.

The identical input at `/api/resolve` yields `bevacizumab-awwb: BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` and `osimertinib: EXACT / CHEMBL3353410`. Bare `bevacizumab-awwb` is refused. Thus the test names a product the repository itself refuses; no external pharmacology assumption is needed. [Actual HTTP responses](m5_http.json), [exact current/predecessor linkage](names_m5_confirm.json).

| Exact `/api/ask` text | Current evidence |
|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib?` | 12 rows, first `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib?` | 12 rows, first `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |

**Cause:** the splitter at `src/query.py:128-130` leaves `/` attached to the word; listing symbols are recognized only as whole tokens (`145-149`). `RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX` is anchored to the end of the string (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213-223`), so the extra slash defeats it. No contiguous *word* run removes that slash. Alias matching then calls `canonical_key`, which discards punctuation (`src/textrules.py:339-346`; `src/query.py:182-197`), recovering the originator alias after the guard missed it. `Engine.explore` trusts that hit (`src/app.py:88-95`).

`bevacizumab - awwb/ osimertinib` and `bevacizumab-awwb| osimertinib` also leak over HTTP. With spaces on both sides of `/`, the guard recognizes the suffix and HEAD refuses the entire item; semicolon or `and` correctly preserves only osimertinib. The whole-item slash refusal is accepted under the user's rule, not counted as over-refusal. Exact `bf436c6` comparison reproduces the attached-slash leak: **inherited, not introduced by HEAD**.

**Required outcome:** punctuation that alias normalization removes must not hide a refusal. Never map or answer for CHEMBL1201583 from this refused component. Retaining the separately recognized osimertinib or refusing an ambiguous combined item can both be safe outcomes; selecting BEVACIZUMAB cannot.

### M5-B2 — P1: a listing word inside one product description detaches its qualifier

Exact input **`liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin`** returns HTTP 200 with **DOXORUBICIN / CHEMBL53463**, **3 trials**, and `also:[{"refused":"liposomal"}]`. The full-string resolver returns a single **BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT** component with no accepted drug. `and` joins descriptions of one formulation here; it does not introduce another named drug. [HTTP](m5_http.json), [independent reproduction](ui_boundary_probe.json).

`Which trials evaluate liposomal and nanoparticle formulations of doxorubicin?` returns **3 originator trial rows**, beginning `NCT01970540|EVALUATES|CHEMBL53463|NCT01970540`. The singular QA version independently yields the same three rows. The singular/plural map forms and `liposomal and sustained release doxorubicin` also fail. [Actual QA](m5_http.json), [independent singular QA](ui_boundary_probe.json).

**Cause:** `_JOINERS` (`src/query.py:94-95`) and the unconditional boundary at `146-149` isolate `liposomal` before examining the drug. The scan at `156-164` only considers words within one resulting item; nothing in the second item matches the current refusal patterns, so DOXORUBICIN remains a positive hit (`190-198`). The full product predicate recognizes the original phrase via `liposomal` (`src/textrules.py:148,207-208`). The fail-closed rule cannot protect a qualifier the splitter has already detached.

This is a different mechanism from the attached-punctuation bypass. It also occurs with the exact previous linker, so it is **inherited**, now confirmed against the changed design. Ordinary `liposomal doxorubicin and osimertinib` is not the requested failure case: separate named products should retain their isolation. No over-refusal of a valid same-item drug is alleged here.

**Required outcome:** a conjunction inside a single qualified product must not make its originator acceptable. If such a phrase cannot be resolved safely, refuse its drug. Preserve the successful separate-drug mixed-input controls.

## Should-fix findings, separate from the approval blockers

### M5-S1 — P2: alias matching crosses the very list boundaries used for refusal

Two complementary effects share a cause:

1. **A valid drug alone in its own item disappears.** `biosimilar anastrozole, Arimidex` returns only `refused:'biosimilar anastrozole'`. Its second item is a valid standalone alias: `Arimidex` and `biosimilar anastrozole and Arimidex` both select **ANASTROZOLE / CHEMBL1399**. The resolver gives a refused first component and `Arimidex: ALIAS / CHEMBL1399`; the exact predecessor retains Arimidex. Current comma QA has **0** evidence versus standalone Arimidex's **2** rows. `biosimilar Prolia, xgeva` similarly loses the separately listed Xgeva, while Xgeva alone returns DENOSUMAB / CHEMBL1237023 and **3** trial rows. These are actual CLI HTTP reproductions. [HTTP](cross_item_http.json), [prior/current comparison](names_m5_confirm.json), [extra controls](names_cross_item_refusal.json).
2. **A different salt identity is assembled from two items.** `Erlotinib, hydrochloride biosimilar bevacizumab` now selects **ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742**, **31 trials**, while declaring the second item refused. The exact predecessor and current full resolver preserve first-item **ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553**, **118 trials**. The semicolon form repeats the wrong-salt selection; the `and` form preserves the parent. Trial QA returns 12 salt rows, first `NCT00445848|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1079742|NCT00445848`. This is a constructed list-contract test, not a claim that hydrochloride bevacizumab is a marketed product. [HTTP](m5_http.json), [comparison](names_m5_confirm.json).

`_drug_spans` receives only listing-word positions to skip (`src/query.py:155`); it has no clause/segment boundaries (`231-248`). Aggressive keys join `anastrozole Arimidex` into the stored `anastrozole (arimidex)` alias, or `Erlotinib hydrochloride` across a comma. Line **165** assigns the entire span using only `seg[a]`. A qualifier in its first item refuses tokens in the second; a qualifier in its second item misses the span assigned to the first. The final positive matcher also joins windows without segment checks (`174-198`). This is **new versus bf436c6** for the cited inputs.

The Arimidex/Xgeva cases satisfy the user's explicit should-fix exception: the valid alias is **alone in its own item**. They are not ordinary intended same-item over-refusals. Keep both refusal discovery and accepted-name matching within the same item boundaries; do not borrow words from a refused item to build another identity.

### M5-S2 — P2: an intrinsic qualifier rejects a valid lone stored alias

**`Pegylated interferon alfa-2a`** is a unique stored ChEMBL synonym of **CHEMBL1201560 / PEGINTERFERON ALFA-2A**. Used alone, HEAD refuses it. The canonical name `PEGINTERFERON ALFA-2A` and exact node ID both draw that live drug. The previous linker reached the same drug through residual `alfa-2a`, accompanied by an incomplete refusal; HEAD now produces no map. Of 7,511 unambiguous alias keys, this is the only newly refused previously linked lone alias. [Stored alias/comparison](names_m5_summary.json), [HTTP controls](m5_http.json).

The full resolver already blocks the synonym via `pegylated`, so this is a **shared dictionary/guard inconsistency**, not a new resolver disagreement. The alias names the stored pegylated drug itself; no originator substitution is demonstrated. It is should-fix under the explicit lone-valid-alias test, and does not block a demo. Do not generalize this into accepting every known alias before product guards: the B1 suffix alias shows why such an exemption would be unsafe. Relevant logic: `src/textrules.py:148,207-208`; `src/query.py:153-168`.

### M5-S3 — P2: all-substring refusal scan creates substantial input cost under the global lock

For an unqualified item containing n tokens, `src/query.py:161-163` tries n(n+1)/2 word runs. Constructing and scanning their strings incurs cubic aggregate character work for equal-length tokens. The scan runs even without any drug. Instrumented exact-predecessor/current runs use the same inputs/data; times are local observations, not universal performance guarantees. [Reproducer](perf_probe.py), [results](perf_results.json).

| Exact constructed input | Characters | bf436c6 linker | HEAD linker |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `" ".join(["x"] * 50)` | 99 | 0.0012 s | 0.0220 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 100)` | 199 | 0.0022 s | 0.1332 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 200)` | 399 | 0.0046 s | 0.9778 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 250)` | 499 | 0.0064 s | 1.7907 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 500)` — direct API | 999 | 0.0116 s | 18.3512 s |
| `" ".join(["x"] * 1000)` — direct API | 1,999 | 0.0249 s | worker exceeded 20 s; stopped |

At 250 tokens HEAD makes **31,625 calls to each predicate**, processing **10,479,750 aggregate characters** across the two predicates, versus 250 calls each / 500 characters in the predecessor. A single 500-character word takes 0.0073 s; 250 semicolon-separated x items take 0.0102 s; a late biosimilar qualifier short-circuits at 0.0225 s. This is token/run growth, not simply request byte length.

Uninstrumented HTTP confirmation: the allowed 499-character input took **1.8472 s** in `/api/explore`, **2.2104 s** in `/api/ask`; adding a valid osimertinib within 499 characters took **2.1657 s**. Four simultaneous allowed inputs completed in **7.9315 s**, with individual latencies up to **7.9215 s**. A normal EGFR request dispatched 0.1 s after one long lookup took **1.8568 s**, versus **0.0366 s** after it completed. The actual server holds `engine.lock` during linking (`src/app.py:247-263`). A 501-character text is promptly rejected, as intended. [Raw HTTP measurements](perf_http.json).

The 500-character text cap does not bound every caller: `/api/check` allows a 4,000-character answer (`src/app.py:260`), which is relinked by `check_groundedness` (`src/query.py:605-608`). With `{"text":"EGFR","answer":" ".join(["x"]*500)}`, the 999-character answer exceeded a **5-second** client timeout; the following ordinary EGFR map exceeded its **2-second** timeout while GET `/` still succeeded. The owned process was then terminated. These are bounded observations, **not a completed latency or permanent-deadlock claim**. [Exact payload/results](perf_check_http.json), [cleanup](perf_check_lifecycle.json).

Preserve refusal coverage while avoiding exhaustive reconstruction of every word run, and cover the answer-field caller when bounding work. This is should-fix performance evidence, separate from originator leaks.

- `Which trials evaluate osimertinib and biosimilar bevacizumab?` retains 12 osimertinib rows; the corresponding sponsor question starts AstraZeneca→SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF→CHEMBL3353410. `EGFR biosimilar bevacizumab` and `AstraZeneca biosimilar bevacizumab` retain the target/company. The same-item `osimertinib with generic version of bevacizumab` now correctly refuses both drugs. [HTTP](m5_http.json), [m4 replay](attack_http.json).
- Tested missed separators include newline/tab, slash/pipe, bullets, Arabic comma/semicolon, halfwidth ideographic comma, full stops and phrases such as `as well as`. When they leave multiple drugs in one item and the qualifier is detected, the resulting whole-item refusal is **not reported as should-fix**. The attached-suffix leak is distinct: it accepts the forbidden originator. [113 cases](names_m5_attack.json).
- Graph snapshot: 7 retired nodes, 142 retired edges, no live edges with retired endpoints and no self-loops; all six node types covered. Trial phase/status/start/title/url, sponsor count/class, Drug global count/max phase, action and association score match stored values. PARENT_OF, STUDIES, ASSOCIATED_WITH, EVALUATES, ACTS_ON and role-aware SPONSORS directions agree with stored endpoints. [Exhaustive evidence](db_validate.json), [snapshot](db_snapshot_facts.json).
- A 1,001-Drug fixture crosses three count batches and returns every item with count/weight 1. Ordinary Disease HTTP returns 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 94.144 ms**. Original **24 requests / 12 workers** all return expected 200 shapes in **556.975 ms**. These ordinary local samples are separate from the long-input tests. [Fixtures](db_fixtures.json), [HTTP](http_results.json).
- README command wording remains accurate at `README.md:566` / `README.ko.md:387`. The detailed map promises still fail for B1/B2 (`src/app.py:74-76`; `web/try.html:215`). No new wrong edge, relation direction, stored value or executable-markup path was identified in the exercised map paths; browser-executed XSS remains unverified.

17:         start=time.perf_counter(); ents=e.kg.link_entities(value); times.append(time.perf_counter()-start)
18:     start=time.perf_counter(); result=e.check('EGFR',value); check=time.perf_counter()-start
19:     rows.append({'name':name,'text':value,'characters':len(value),'link_seconds':times,
20:                  'link_median_seconds':statistics.median(times),'check_seconds':check,
21:                  'entity_count':len(ents),'evidence_count':result['evidence_count']})
22: e.kg.conn.close()
23: (OUT/'cost_stress.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
24: print(json.dumps([{k:v for k,v in r.items() if k not in ('text','link_seconds')} for r in rows],indent=2))

FILE src/query.py
1: """Grounded question answering over the graph.
2: 
3: The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
4: question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
5: graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
6: dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.
7: 
8: Three properties the answering layer holds to:
9: 
10: * **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
11:   each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
12: * **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
13:   the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
14:   against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
15:   graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
16:   directly.
17: * **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
18:   reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
19: """
20: from __future__ import annotations
21: 
22: import json
23: import os
24: import re
25: import sqlite3
26: import sys
27: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
28: from pathlib import Path
29: 
30: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
31: import config as C
32: import normalize as N
33: import textrules as T
34: 
35: DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
36: TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"
37: 
38: 
39: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
40: # evidence
41: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
42: @dataclass
43: class Evidence:
44:     statement: str
45:     edge_id: str
46:     relation: str
47:     source: str
48:     source_record: str | None
49:     fetched_at: str | None
50:     confidence: float
51:     tier: str | None = None
52:     url: str | None = None
53:     detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
54: 
55:     def citation(self) -> str:
56:         base = self.source_record or self.source
57:         return f"[{base}]"
58: 
59: 
60: @dataclass
61: class Answer:
62:     question: str
63:     intent: str
64:     entities: list[dict]
65:     text: str
66:     evidence: list[Evidence]
67:     generated_by: str
68:     checks: dict
69: 
70:     def to_dict(self) -> dict:
71:         return {
72:             "question": self.question,
73:             "intent": self.intent,
74:             "entities": self.entities,
75:             "answer": self.text,
76:             "generated_by": self.generated_by,
77:             "checks": self.checks,
78:             "evidence": [
79:                 {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
80:                  "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
81:                  "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
82:                  "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
83:                 for e in self.evidence
84:             ],
85:         }
86: 
87: 
88: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
89: # engine
90: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
91: # Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
92: # them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
93: # nanoparticles").
94: _JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
95:             "or/and", "and/or"}
96: 
97: 
98: 
99: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
100:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
101: 
102:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
103:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
104:     work grows linearly with the item."""
105:     text = " ".join(words)
106:     if T.is_distinct_product(text) or T.is_radiotracer(text):
107:         return True
108:     return any(T.is_distinct_product(w) or T.is_radiotracer(w)
109:                for w in (" ".join(words[i:j]) for i in range(len(words))
110:                          for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1)))
111: 
112: class OncoKG:
113:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
114:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
115:         self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
116:         self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
117:         resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
118:         self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
119:         self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
120:         self.disease = resolved["disease"]
121:         self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
122:                         if t.get("symbol")}
123:         # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
124:         # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
125:         self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
126:         for row in self.conn.execute(
127:                 "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
128:             self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
129:             self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
130:         # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
131:         for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
132:             if same in self.companies:
133:                 self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
134:         self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}
135: 
136:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
137:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
138:         blocked = self._resolver_blocked(question)
139:         whole = self._whole_name(question, blocked)
140:         if whole:
141:             return [whole]
142:         found = self._link(question)
143:         if not blocked:
144:             return found
145:         # a drug the resolver refuses in this text is refused here too, whatever the
146:         # splitting below made of it (the two must agree before anything is linked)
147:         out = [e for e in found if not (e["type"] == "Drug" and e["id"] in blocked)]
148:         for text in sorted({t for t in blocked.values()}):
149:             if not any(e["type"] == "Refused" and (text in e["label"] or e["label"] in text)
150:                        for e in out):
151:                 out.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text, "matched_text": text,
152:                             "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not the "
153:                                    "originator molecule"})
154:         return out
155: 
156:     def _resolver_blocked(self, text: str) -> dict:
157:         """Drug ids inside the pieces the intervention resolver refuses as a distinct
158:         product or a tracer, with the refused piece."""
159:         comps = []
160:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;������/|]", text):  # the list pieces used below
161:             if not piece.strip():
162:                 continue
163:             try:
164:                 comps += N.resolve_drug_string(piece, self.dic, self.index_keys)["components"]
165:             except Exception:                               # never let a guard crash a lookup
166:                 continue
167:         out: dict = {}
168:         accepted = {c.get("drug_id") for c in comps
169:                     if c.get("drug_id") and not str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED")}
170:         for c in comps:
171:             piece = c.get("component") or ""
172:             if not (c.get("tier") == "BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT"
173:                     or (str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED") and T.is_radiotracer(piece))):
174:                 continue
175:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[\s/|,;]+", piece) if w]
176:             words = [w.strip(" ?.!:()[]") or w for w in words]
177:             for a, b in self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
178:                 key = T.canonical_key(" ".join(words[a:b]))
179:                 for h in self.dic["index"].get(key, []):
180:                     # the same molecule named separately and accepted ("biosimilar
181:                     # anastrozole, Arimidex") is left to the list-item rule below
182:                     if h["drug_id"] not in accepted:
183:                         out[h["drug_id"]] = piece
184:         return out
185: 
186:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict):
187:         """The whole input is one stored drug name ("CP-358,774", "Rapamycin,
188:         42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-"): read it whole, before list punctuation splits it."""
189:         if re.search(r"[/|]", text) or any(w.lower() in _JOINERS for w in text.split()):
190:             return None                              # a list, not one name
191:         key = T.canonical_key(text.strip(" ?.!"))
192:         hits = self.dic["index"].get(key) if key else None
193:         if not hits or len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) != 1 or blocked:
194:             return None
195:         if T.is_distinct_product(text) or T.is_radiotracer(text):
196:             return None
197:         did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
198:         return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
199:                 "matched_text": text.strip(), "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]}
200: 
201:     def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
202:         found: list[dict] = []
203:         seen: set = set()
204:         tokens: list[str] = []
205:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
206:         inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
207:         ci, depth = 0, 0
208:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
209:             for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
210:                 # a slash or bar, glued or free, ends the piece ("bevacizumab - awwb/
211:                 # osimertinib"); it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
212:                 for j, part in enumerate(re.split(r"[/|]", raw)):
213:                     if j:
214:                         ci += 1
215:                     if not part:
216:                         continue
217:                     if re.search(r"\w", part):
218:                         # edge punctuation off, as the resolver does ("bevacizumab-
219:                         # awwb\u3002"); brackets stay, they mark isotopes ("[89Zr]")
220:                         t = re.sub(r"^[^\w\[\(]+|[^\w\]\)]+$", "", part)
221:                     elif part in ("-", "\u2013", "\u2014", "+", "&"):
222:                         t = part            # "bevacizumab - awwb" needs its dash
223:                     else:
224:                         t = ""
225:                     if t:
226:                         tokens.append(t)
227:                         clause.append(ci)
228:                         inner.append(depth > 0 or t.startswith("("))
229:                     depth = max(0, depth + part.count("(") - part.count(")"))
230:             ci += 1
231: 
232:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
233:         # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
234:         # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
235:         # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
236:         # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
237:         # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
238:         # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
239:         # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
240:         seg: list[int] = []
241:         n = 0
242:         for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
243:             # "and" inside parentheses describes one product ("doxorubicin
244:             # (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)"); it does not list two
245:             if t.lower() in _JOINERS and not inner[k]:
246:                 n += 1
247:                 seg.append(-1)
248:                 continue
249:             if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
250:                 n += 1
251:             seg.append(n)
252:         refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
253:                              if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
254:         drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
255:         dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
256:         prev_end = -1
257:         for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
258:             idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
259:             text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
260:             joined = dangling is not None and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0])) \
261:                 and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
262:             prev_end = idx[-1]
263:             hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
264:             if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
265:                 named = any(k in refused and (
266:                     T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
267:                     or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
268:                     for k in idx)
269:                 if not hit and not named:
270:                     # "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": a bare
271:                     # qualifier carries over a listing word to the next item's drug; a
272:                     # product named by itself ("Mvasi and bevacizumab") does not
273:                     dangling = text
274:                     continue
275:                 dangling = None
276:             elif joined and hit:
277:                 text = f"{dangling} ... {text}"
278:                 dangling = None
279:             else:
280:                 dangling = None if not joined else dangling
281:                 continue
282:             refused.update(hit)
283:             found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
284:                           "matched_text": text,
285:                           "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
286:                                  "the originator molecule"})
287: 
288:         matched: set[int] = set()
289:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
290:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
291:                 if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
292:                     continue
293:                 if size > 1 and (len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1 or min(seg[i:i + size]) < 0):
294:                     continue            # a name never spans two list items
295:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
296:                 if len(window) < 3:
297:                     continue
298:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
299: 
300:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
301:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
302:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
303:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
304:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
305:                     hits = None
306:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
307:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
308:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
309:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
310:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
311:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
312:                                       "matched_text": window,
313:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
314:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
315:                     continue
316: 
317:                 if window.lower() in self.targets:
318:                     t = self.targets[window.lower()]
319:                     if ("Target", t["target_id"]) not in seen:
320:                         seen.add(("Target", t["target_id"]))
321:                         found.append({"type": "Target", "id": t["target_id"],
322:                                       "label": t["symbol"], "matched_text": window})
323:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
324:                     continue
325: 
326:                 if key in self.companies:
327:                     cid = self.companies[key][0]
328:                     if ("Company", cid) not in seen:
329:                         seen.add(("Company", cid))
330:                         row = self.conn.execute(

71:     (OUT / "regression_http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(regression.stdout + regression.stderr)
72:     assert regression.returncode == 0
73: 
74:     # Load the previous linker/answer module in memory; use the identical database.
75:     old = types.ModuleType("m2b_old_query")
76:     old.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
77:     sys.modules[old.__name__] = old
78:     code = subprocess.run(["git", "show", "bfcb83d:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT,
79:                           capture_output=True, check=True).stdout.decode("utf-8")
80:     exec(compile(code, old.__file__, "exec"), old.__dict__)
81:     baseline = old.OncoKG()
82:     baseline.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
83:     html = (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
84:     questions = json.loads(re.search(r"const EX2 = (\[.*?\]);", html, re.S).group(1))
85:     # Verify CLI/dashboard demos are covered by the page list, without running
86:     # their entrypoints (which write tracked report/dashboard artifacts).
87:     sources = {}
88:     for filename, variable in [("src/query.py", "questions"),
89:                                ("src/make_dashboard_data.py", "demo_questions")]:
90:         parsed = ast.parse((ROOT / filename).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
91:         assignments = [n for n in ast.walk(parsed) if isinstance(n, ast.Assign)
92:                        and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == variable for t in n.targets)]
93:         value = assignments[-1].value
94:         if isinstance(value, ast.BoolOp):
95:             value = value.values[-1]
96:         sources[filename] = ast.literal_eval(value)
97:         assert set(sources[filename]) <= set(questions)
98:     answers = []
99:     for question in questions:
100:         previous = baseline.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
101:         current = post("/api/ask", {"text": question})
102:         answers.append({"question": question, "http_status": current["status"],
103:                         "identical_to_bfcb83d": current["body"] == previous,
104:                         "current": current["body"], "previous": previous})
105:     baseline.conn.close()
106:     (OUT / "http_demo_results.json").write_text(json.dumps({"source_lists": sources,
107:         "answers": answers}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
108:     print(json.dumps({"demo_questions": len(answers),
109:         "all_http_200": all(a["http_status"] == 200 for a in answers),
110:         "all_identical_to_bfcb83d": all(a["identical_to_bfcb83d"] for a in answers)}))
111: finally:
112:     proc.terminate()
113:     proc.wait(timeout=10)
114:     log.close()
115:     with socket.socket() as s:
116:         port_closed = s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
117:     after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
118:     (OUT / "http_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
119:         "server_exit": proc.returncode, "port_closed": port_closed,
120:         "database_unchanged": after == before, "db_sha256_before": before,
121:         "db_sha256_after": after}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m6/root_checks/perf_check_http.py
1: """Bounded probe of the answer field passed back into the new linker."""
2: import concurrent.futures
3: import hashlib
4: import json
5: import os
6: from pathlib import Path
7: import socket
8: import subprocess
9: import sys
10: import time
11: import urllib.error
12: import urllib.request
13: 
14: OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
15: ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
16: BASE = 'http://127.0.0.1:8896'
17: DB = ROOT / 'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite'
18: 
19: def post(path, payload, timeout):
20:     started = time.perf_counter()
21:     try:
22:         req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
23:                                      headers={'Content-Type': 'application/json'})
24:         try:
25:             response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout)
26:         except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
27:             response = error
28:         return {'status': response.status, 'body': json.loads(response.read()),
29:                 'seconds': time.perf_counter()-started}
30:     except TimeoutError:
31:         return {'timeout_seconds': timeout, 'seconds': time.perf_counter()-started}
32: 
33: with socket.socket() as sock:
34:     assert sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1', 8896)) != 0
35: before = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
36: env = {**os.environ, 'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE': '1', 'PYTHONUTF8': '1'}
37: with (OUT / 'perf_check_server.txt').open('w', encoding='utf8') as log:
38:     proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, '-B', '-u', 'src/app.py', '8896'], cwd=ROOT,
39:         env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
40:         creationflags=getattr(subprocess, 'CREATE_NO_WINDOW', 0))
41:     try:
42:         for _ in range(100):
43:             assert proc.poll() is None
44:             try:
45:                 urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
46:                 break
47:             except OSError:
48:                 time.sleep(.1)
49:         else:
50:             raise RuntimeError('server unavailable')
51:         normal = post('/api/explore', {'text':'EGFR'}, 5)
52:         small = post('/api/check', {'text':'EGFR', 'answer':' '.join(['x']*100)}, 5)
53:         payload = {'text':'EGFR', 'answer':' '.join(['x']*500)}
54:         slow = post('/api/check', payload, 5)
55:         started = time.perf_counter()
56:         get = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=2)
57:         get.read()
58:         live_get = {'status': get.status, 'seconds': time.perf_counter()-started}
59:         follower = post('/api/explore', {'text':'EGFR'}, 2)
60:         output = {'normal_before': normal, 'small_check_199_characters': small,
61:                   'slow_check_input': payload, 'slow_check_999_characters': slow,
62:                   'get_while_check_running': live_get, 'normal_after_check_timeout': follower,
63:                   'limitation':'Client timeouts bound the observation; not a completed latency or permanent deadlock claim.'}
64:         (OUT / 'perf_check_http.json').write_text(json.dumps(output, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
65:         print(json.dumps({k: ({x:y for x,y in v.items() if x!='body'} if isinstance(v,dict) else v)
66:                           for k,v in output.items() if k!='slow_check_input'}))
67:     finally:
68:         proc.terminate()
69:         proc.wait(timeout=10)
70:         with socket.socket() as sock:
71:             closed = sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1', 8896)) != 0
72:         after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
73:         (OUT / 'perf_check_lifecycle.json').write_text(json.dumps({'pid':proc.pid, 'port':8896,
74:             'port_closed':closed,'database_unchanged':before==after,'server_exit':proc.returncode},indent=2),encoding='utf8')
75:         assert closed and before==after

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m6/root_checks/perf_probe.py
1: """Bounded subprocess benchmarks; writes confined to m5 and never calls a model."""
2: import json
3: import os
4: from pathlib import Path
5: import subprocess
6: import sys
7: import time
8: import types
9: 
10: OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
11: ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
12: sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
13: 
14: if len(sys.argv) > 1:
15:     import query
16:     import textrules as T
17:     mode, kind, size = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], int(sys.argv[3])
18:     module = query
19:     if mode == 'm4':
20:         module = types.ModuleType('perf_m4_query')
21:         module.__file__ = str(ROOT / 'src/query.py')
22:         sys.modules[module.__name__] = module
23:         code = subprocess.run(['git', 'show', 'bf436c6:src/query.py'], cwd=ROOT,
24:                               capture_output=True, check=True).stdout.decode('utf-8')
25:         exec(compile(code, module.__file__, 'exec'), module.__dict__)
26:     kg = module.OncoKG()
27:     kg.conn.execute('PRAGMA query_only=ON')
28:     text = {'plain': ' '.join(['x'] * size),
29:             'drug': ' '.join(['x'] * size + ['osimertinib']),
30:             'split': ';'.join(['x'] * size),
31:             'single': 'x' * size,
32:             'late_qualifier': ' '.join(['x'] * size + ['biosimilar', 'bevacizumab'])}[kind]
33:     calls = {'is_distinct_product': 0, 'is_radiotracer': 0, 'characters': 0}
34:     for name in ['is_distinct_product', 'is_radiotracer']:
35:         original = getattr(T, name)
36:         def wrapped(value, name=name, original=original):
37:             calls[name] += 1
38:             calls['characters'] += len(value)
39:             return original(value)
40:         setattr(T, name, wrapped)
41:     start = time.perf_counter()
42:     entities = kg.link_entities(text)
43:     elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start
44:     kg.conn.close()
45:     print(json.dumps({'mode': mode, 'kind': kind, 'size': size, 'characters': len(text),
46:                      'seconds': elapsed, 'calls': calls, 'entities': entities}))
47: else:
48:     env = {**os.environ, 'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE': '1', 'PYTHONUTF8': '1',
49:            'PYTHONIOENCODING': 'utf-8', 'TEMP': str(OUT / 'tmp'),
50:            'TMP': str(OUT / 'tmp'), 'TMPDIR': str(OUT / 'tmp')}
51:     cases = [('plain', n) for n in [50, 100, 200, 250, 500, 1000]]
52:     cases += [('drug', 244), ('split', 250), ('single', 500), ('late_qualifier', 235)]
53:     rows = []
54:     for kind, size in cases:
55:         for mode in ['m4', 'HEAD']:
56:             try:
57:                 proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-B', __file__, mode, kind, str(size)],
58:                                       cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, timeout=20)
59:                 row = json.loads(proc.stdout) if proc.returncode == 0 else {
60:                     'mode': mode, 'kind': kind, 'size': size, 'error': proc.stderr.decode('utf-8', 'replace')}
61:             except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
62:                 row = {'mode': mode, 'kind': kind, 'size': size, 'timeout_seconds': 20}
63:             rows.append(row)
64:             (OUT / 'perf_results.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
65:             print(json.dumps(row), flush=True)

"""Fresh live HTTP proof of round-6 name-boundary counterexamples; no source edits."""
import hashlib,json,os,socket,subprocess,sys,time,urllib.request,urllib.error
from pathlib import Path
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent/'root_checks'
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
PORT=8897
BASE=f'http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}'
DB=ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite'
def post(path,payload):
    started=time.perf_counter()
    req=urllib.request.Request(BASE+path,data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),headers={'Content-Type':'application/json'})
    try: res=urllib.request.urlopen(req,timeout=15)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e: res=e
    raw=res.read()
    return {'path':path,'payload':payload,'status':res.status,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-started,
            'bytes':len(raw),'body':json.loads(raw)}
before=hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
with socket.socket() as s: assert s.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1',PORT))!=0
env={**os.environ,'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE':'1','PYTHONUTF8':'1'}
with (OUT/'confirm_server.txt').open('w',encoding='utf8') as log:
    proc=subprocess.Popen([sys.executable,'-B','-u','src/app.py',str(PORT)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,
        stdout=log,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,creationflags=getattr(subprocess,'CREATE_NO_WINDOW',0))
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None
            try:
                page=urllib.request.urlopen(BASE,timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError: time.sleep(.1)
        else: raise RuntimeError('server unavailable')
        values=['bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib','bevacizumab - awwb|osimertinib',
                'bevacizumab - awwb/ osimertinib','bevacizumab - awwb / osimertinib',
                'bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib EGFR','bevacizumab - awwb',
                'bevacizumab-awwb\u3002','bevacizumab-awwb\u2014','bevacizumab-awwb-',
                '-bevacizumab-awwb','bevacizumab-awwb.','bevacizumab-awwb',
                '(bevacizumab-awwb)','liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin',
                'Mvasi and bevacizumab','biosimilar anastrozole, Arimidex',
                'Erlotinib, hydrochloride biosimilar bevacizumab']
        rows=[post(path,{'text':v}) for v in values for path in ['/api/explore','/api/resolve']]
        for v in ['bevacizumab - awwb/osimertinib','bevacizumab-awwb\u3002','bevacizumab-awwb.']:
            for q in ['Which trials evaluate {}?','Which companies sponsor {}?']:
                rows.append(post('/api/ask',{'text':q.format(v)}))
        perf=[]
        for n in [250,500,1000,2000]:
            perf.append(post('/api/check',{'text':'EGFR','answer':' '.join(['x']*n)}))
        result={'head':subprocess.check_output(['git','rev-parse','HEAD'],cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),
                'base':BASE,'page_matches_worktree':page==(ROOT/'web/try.html').read_bytes(),
                'cases':rows,'answer_cost':perf}
        (OUT/'confirm_http.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
        for r in rows:
            b=r['body']
            print(json.dumps({'path':r['path'],'text':r['payload']['text'],'status':r['status'],
                              'center':b.get('center',{}).get('id'),'refused':b.get('refused'),
                              'evidence':len(b.get('evidence',[])),
                              'components':[(c.get('status'),c.get('drug_id')) for c in b.get('components',[])]},ensure_ascii=False))
        print('answer_cost',[(len(r['payload']['answer']),r['seconds'],r['status']) for r in perf])
    finally:
        proc.terminate(); proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as s: closed=s.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1',PORT))!=0
        after=hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
        (OUT/'confirm_lifecycle.json').write_text(json.dumps({'pid':proc.pid,'port':PORT,

              'before':before,'after':after},indent=2),encoding='utf8')

"""Fresh live HTTP proof of round-6 name-boundary counterexamples; no source edits."""
import hashlib,json,os,socket,subprocess,sys,time,urllib.request,urllib.error
from pathlib import Path
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent/'root_checks'
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
PORT=8899
BASE=f'http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}'
DB=ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite'
def post(path,payload):
    started=time.perf_counter()
    req=urllib.request.Request(BASE+path,data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),headers={'Content-Type':'application/json'})
    try: res=urllib.request.urlopen(req,timeout=15)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e: res=e
    raw=res.read()
    return {'path':path,'payload':payload,'status':res.status,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-started,
            'bytes':len(raw),'body':json.loads(raw)}
before=hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
with socket.socket() as s: assert s.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1',PORT))!=0
env={**os.environ,'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE':'1','PYTHONUTF8':'1'}
with (OUT/'alias_server.txt').open('w',encoding='utf8') as log:
    proc=subprocess.Popen([sys.executable,'-B','-u','src/app.py',str(PORT)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,
        stdout=log,stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,creationflags=getattr(subprocess,'CREATE_NO_WINDOW',0))
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None
            try:

    raw=res.read()
    return {'path':path,'payload':payload,'status':res.status,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-started,

159: # Words that follow a drug name after a hyphen but are not suffixes at all.

162:                      "naive", "based", "alone", "mono", "plus", "arm"}
163: 
164: # Since 2017 the FDA gives a four-letter suffix to *originator* biologics as
165: # well (cemiplimab-rwlc, dostarlimab-gxly, tislelizumab-jsgr), so a suffix on
166: # its own does not mark a biosimilar. Treating every suffix as one blocked the
167: # originators. A four-letter lowercase suffix is a biosimilar only when it is a
168: # known biosimilar suffix; a longer or capitalised suffix is a sponsor name.
169: BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES = {

202: def is_distinct_product(text: str) -> bool:
203:     """True when the string names a product that is not the originator."""
204:     t = (text or "").strip()
205:     if not t:
206:         return False
207:     if RE_DISTINCT_PRODUCT.search(t):
208:         return True
209:     words = set(re.findall(r"[a-z]+", t.lower()))
210:     words |= {canonical_key(w) for w in re.findall(r"[A-Za-z]+[\s\-]?\d+", t)}
211:     if words & (BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | REFORMULATION_NAMES):
212:         return True
213:     m = RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX.match(t)
214:     if not m:
215:         return False
216:     suffix = m.group("suffix")
217:     if suffix.lower() in SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES:
218:         return False
219:     # an FDA suffix is four letters, written in either case ("-rwlc", "-RWLC");
220:     # a title-case word is a sponsor name ("-Pfizer", "-Teva")
221:     if len(suffix) == 4 and (suffix.islower() or suffix.isupper()):
222:         return suffix.lower() in BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES
223:     return True
224: 
225: 
226: # An originator's FDA suffix carries no information about the molecule, so it
227: # is removed before lookup: "cemiplimab-rwlc" -> "cemiplimab".
228: RE_FDA_SUFFIX = re.compile(
229:     r"^(?P<inn>[A-Za-z]{6,}(?:mab|cept|ase|grastim|stim|poetin))\s*-\s*(?P<sfx>[A-Za-z]{4})$")

330:     fired = False
331:     for w in sorted(words, key=len, reverse=True):
332:         pattern = re.compile(rf"\b{re.escape(w)}\b", re.I)

339: def canonical_key(text: str) -> str:
340:     """Aggressive key used for dictionary lookup (never shown to a user)."""
341:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
342:     t = "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c))
343:     t = t.lower()
344:     t = t.replace("’", "'").replace("‐", "-").replace("–", "-").replace("—", "-")
345:     t = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "", t)
346:     return t
