348: 
349: def clean_surface(raw: str) -> dict:
350:     """Clean one intervention string.
351: 
352:     Returns the cleaned text, the parenthetical fragments pulled out as extra
353:     lookup candidates, and the list of rules that fired.
354:     """
355:     rules: list[str] = []
356:     text = (raw or "").strip()
357: 
358:     parentheticals = [part.strip()
359:                       for groups in RE_PAREN.findall(text)
360:                       for part in groups if part and part.strip()]
361:     if parentheticals:
362:         rules.append("extract_parenthetical")
363:         text = RE_PAREN.sub(" ", text)
364: 
365:     for name, rx in (
366:         ("strip_dose", RE_DOSE),
367:         ("strip_percent", RE_PERCENT),
368:         ("strip_schedule", RE_SCHEDULE),
369:         ("strip_cycle", RE_CYCLE),
370:     ):
371:         if rx.search(text):
372:             text = rx.sub(" ", text)
373:             rules.append(name)
374: 
375:     text, fired = _strip_words(text, FORMULATIONS)
376:     if fired:
377:         rules.append("strip_formulation")
378:     text, fired = _strip_words(text, REGIMEN_WORDS)
379:     if fired:
380:         rules.append("strip_regimen_word")
381: 
382:     text = RE_MULTISPACE.sub(" ", text)
383:     text = RE_PUNCT_EDGE.sub("", text)
384: 
385:     return {"cleaned": text, "parentheticals": parentheticals, "rules": rules}

388: # A phosphate or succinate on a corticosteroid, etoposide or fludarabine is a
389: # covalent ester -- a prodrug with its own record -- not a counter-ion, so
390: # stripping it as a salt changed the molecule ("prednisolone phosphate" ->
391: # PREDNISOLONE). These forms are left whole and resolve only if the
392: # vocabulary lists them as written.
393: RE_ESTER_FORM = re.compile(
394:     r"\b(?:prednisolone|methylprednisolone|hydrocortisone|dexamethasone|"
395:     r"betamethasone|triamcinolone|etoposide|fludarabine)\b"
396:     r"(?:\s+sodium)?\s+(?:phosphate|succinate|acetate)\b", re.I)

530:     for tok in re.split(r"[\s\-/,:()]+", text):

544: 
545: MAX_SEGMENT_WINDOW = 6
546: 
547: 
548: def smart_tokens(text: str, index: dict) -> list[str]:
549:     """Tokenise without destroying development codes.
550: 
551:     'BMS-986504' must stay one token while 'Docetaxel-Carboplatin' must become
552:     two. The dictionary itself decides: a hyphenated token is only split when it
553:     is neither a development code nor a term the dictionary already knows.
554:     """
555:     out: list[str] = []
556:     for tok in re.split(r"[\s/;]+", text):
557:         tok = tok.strip(" ,:()[]")
558:         if not tok:
559:             continue
560:         if "-" in tok and not T.is_dev_code(tok) and T.canonical_key(tok) not in index:
561:             parts = [p for p in tok.split("-") if p]
562:             out.extend(parts if len(parts) > 1 else [tok])
563:         else:
564:             out.append(tok)
565:     return out
566: 
567: 
568: def segment_aliases(index: dict, text: str):
569:     """Greedy longest-match segmentation of a phrase against the dictionary.
570: 
571:     'Cisplatin Docetaxel Gefitinib Pemetrexed Vinorelbine Gemcitabine' has no
572:     separator at all, yet names six substances. Matching only the first one and
573:     discarding the rest would produce a record that looks complete and is not --
574:     the most dangerous kind of error, because nothing downstream can detect it.
575:     """
576:     tokens = smart_tokens(text, index)
577:     found: list[dict] = []
578:     leftover: list[str] = []
579:     i = 0
580:     while i < len(tokens):
581:         matched = False
582:         # "18F-thymidine": the token after an isotope label is the tracer moiety,
583:         # not an administered drug, so it must not be matched on its own.
584:         if i > 0 and T.is_isotope_label(tokens[i - 1]):
585:             leftover.append(tokens[i])
586:             i += 1
587:             continue
588:         for size in range(min(MAX_SEGMENT_WINDOW, len(tokens) - i), 0, -1):
589:             window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
590:             key = T.canonical_key(window)
591:             if len(key) < MIN_CONTAINED_LEN or key in GENERIC_FOR_CONTAINED:
592:                 continue
593:             hits = index.get(key)
594:             if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
595:                 found.append({"window": window, "hits": hits})
596:                 i += size
597:                 matched = True
598:                 break
599:         if not matched:
600:             leftover.append(tokens[i])
601:             i += 1
602:     return found, leftover
603: 
604: 
605: def _raw_parts_by_cleaned(raw: str, parts: list[str]) -> dict[str, str]:
606:     """Map each cleaned combination part back to the raw text it came from."""
607:     if len(parts) <= 1:
608:         return {}
609:     raw_parts = T.split_combination(raw)
610:     out: dict[str, str] = {}
611:     for rp in raw_parts:
612:         cleaned = T.clean_surface(rp)["cleaned"] or rp
613:         for cp in T.split_combination(cleaned):
614:             ck = T.canonical_key(cp)
615:             for p in parts:
616:                 if p not in out and T.canonical_key(p) == ck:
617:                     out[p] = rp
618:     return out

621: def target_guard_keys(target_rows: list[dict], dic: dict) -> set[str]:
622:     """Names of targets, so a gene symbol in an intervention is refused, not matched."""
623:     keys = set()
624:     for r in target_rows:
625:         t = r["target"]
626:         for label in [t.get("approvedSymbol"), t.get("approvedName")]:
627:             if label:
628:                 keys.add(T.canonical_key(label))
629:         for grp in ("synonyms", "symbolSynonyms", "nameSynonyms", "obsoleteSymbols"):
630:             for a in (t.get(grp) or []):
631:                 if a.get("label"):
632:                     keys.add(T.canonical_key(a["label"]))
633:     return keys - set(dic["index"].keys())   # a term that is also a drug stays a drug

636: def resolve_drug_string(raw: str, dic: dict, index_keys: list[str],
637:                         target_keys: set | None = None) -> dict:
638:     """Resolve one intervention string into one or more component resolutions."""
639:     index = dic["index"]
640:     out = {"raw": raw, "components": []}
641: 
642:     cleaned_doc = T.clean_surface(raw)
643:     base = cleaned_doc["cleaned"] or raw
644:     parts = T.split_combination(base)
645:     if len(parts) > 1:
646:         cleaned_doc["rules"].append("split_combination")
647: 
648:     # A placebo-controlled arm is not a drug-free arm. "placebo plus docetaxel"
649:     # really did administer docetaxel, while "placebo to match ZD6474" did not
650:     # administer anything. Blocking the whole string is safe for the second case
651:     # and silently destroys a true exposure in the first, so the two are split:
652:     # a placebo joined by a combination separator means co-administration, a
653:     # placebo naming its reference drug does not.
654:     if T.is_placebo(raw):
655:         # Only a single-component string is a whole reference arm. In a
656:         # combination, "cisplatin + placebo to match pembrolizumab" really did
657:         # give cisplatin; the placebo part is blocked on its own below.
658:         reference_arm = len(parts) == 1
659:         if reference_arm:
660:             out["components"].append({
661:                 "component": raw, "tier": TIER_BLOCKED_PLACEBO, "drug_id": None,
662:                 "score": None, "rules": ["placebo_guard", "placebo_reference_arm"],
663:                 "note": "placebo arm naming its reference drug - no exposure",
664:             })
665:             return out
666:         cleaned_doc["rules"].append("placebo_co_administration_split")
667: 
668:     # drugs a placebo part says it imitates ("placebo to match X" -> X)
669:     referenced: set[str] = set()

674:                 target = T.RE_PLACEBO.sub(" ", target).strip(" ,;:-")
675:                 if target and target != p:
676:                     for c in resolve_drug_string(target, dic, index_keys,
677:                                                  target_keys)["components"]:
678:                         if c.get("drug_id"):
679:                             referenced.add(c["drug_id"])
680: 
681:     # Each cleaned part is paired with the uncleaned text it came from. Cleaning
682:     # removes "liposomal" as a formulation word, so the distinct-product guard
683:     # on a cleaned part could not see it: "liposomal doxorubicin" was blocked
684:     # alone and accepted as plain doxorubicin once "and cisplatin" was added.
685:     # The same pairing keeps a bracketed code attached to its own component.
686:     raw_of = _raw_parts_by_cleaned(raw, parts)

+"""Bounded, same-data cost comparison and owned CLI lock-contention check."""
+import concurrent.futures
+import hashlib
+import json
+import os
+from pathlib import Path
+import socket
+import statistics
+import subprocess
+import sys
+import time
+import types
+import urllib.error
+import urllib.request
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
+PORT = 8902
+BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
+
+
+def dump(name, obj):
+    (OUT / name).write_text(json.dumps(obj, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf8")
+
+
+if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == "worker":
+    import query
+    ref, kind, size = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], int(sys.argv[4])
+    mod = query
+    if ref != "HEAD":
+        mod = types.ModuleType("cost_previous")
+        mod.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
+        sys.modules[mod.__name__] = mod
+        code = subprocess.check_output(["git", "show", f"{ref}:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT).decode("utf8")
+        exec(compile(code, mod.__file__, "exec"), mod.__dict__)
+    kg = mod.OncoKG()
+    kg.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
+    value = {"plain": " ", "split": ";", "slash": "/"}[kind].join(["x"] * size)
+    times = []
+    for _ in range(3):
+        start = time.perf_counter()
+        entities = kg.link_entities(value)
+        times.append(time.perf_counter() - start)
+    detail = {}
+    if ref == "HEAD":
+        for name in ["_resolver_blocked", "_link"]:
+            start = time.perf_counter()
+            result = getattr(kg, name)(value)
+            detail[name] = {"seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "result_count": len(result)}
+    kg.conn.close()
+    print(json.dumps({"ref": ref, "kind": kind, "size": size, "characters": len(value),
+                      "times": times, "median": statistics.median(times),
+                      "entities": entities, "stages": detail}))
+    raise SystemExit(0)
+
+
+def post(path, payload, timeout=20):
+    start = time.perf_counter()
+    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
+                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
+    try:
+        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout)
+    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
+        response = error
+    raw = response.read()
+    return {"path": path, "payload": payload, "status": response.status,
+            "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "bytes": len(raw), "body": json.loads(raw)}
+
+
+env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
+       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TEMP": str(OUT / "tmp"),
+       "TMP": str(OUT / "tmp"), "TMPDIR": str(OUT / "tmp")}
+rows = []
+for kind, size in [("plain", 250), ("split", 250), ("split", 1000), ("split", 2000)]:
+    for ref in ["b7a00f9", "HEAD"]:
+        try:
+            result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", __file__, "worker", ref, kind, str(size)],
+                                    cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, timeout=35)
+            assert result.returncode == 0, result.stderr.decode("utf8", "replace")
+            row = json.loads(result.stdout)
+        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
+            row = {"ref": ref, "kind": kind, "size": size, "timeout_seconds": 35}
+        rows.append(row)
+        dump("fresh_cost_comparison.json", rows)
+        print(json.dumps(row), flush=True)
+
+db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
+before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+with socket.socket() as sock:
+    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port occupied"
+with (OUT / "fresh_cost_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf8") as log:
+    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
+                            env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
+                            creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
+    try:
+        for _ in range(100):
+            assert proc.poll() is None
+            try:
+                urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
+                break
+            except OSError:
+                time.sleep(.1)
+        else:
+            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
+        baseline = post("/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+        value = ";".join(["x"] * 250)
+        sequential = [post(path, {"text": value}) for path in ["/api/explore", "/api/ask"]]
+        answer = ";".join(["x"] * 2000)
+        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
+            started = time.perf_counter()
+            leader = pool.submit(post, "/api/check", {"text": "EGFR", "answer": answer})
+            time.sleep(.1)
+            dispatched = time.perf_counter() - started
+            follower = pool.submit(post, "/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+            get_start = time.perf_counter()
+            response = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=3)
+            response.read()
+            live_get = {"status": response.status, "seconds": time.perf_counter() - get_start}
+            check, queued = leader.result(), follower.result()
+        after_request = post("/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+        dump("fresh_cost_http.json", {"baseline": baseline, "sequential": sequential,
+             "check": check, "queued_egfr": queued, "follower_dispatched_seconds": dispatched,
+             "live_get": live_get, "normal_after": after_request})
+        print(json.dumps({"check_seconds": check["seconds"], "queued_egfr_seconds": queued["seconds"],
+                          "normal_after_seconds": after_request["seconds"]}), flush=True)
+    finally:
+        proc.terminate()
+        proc.wait(timeout=10)
+        with socket.socket() as sock:
+            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
+        after = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+        dump("fresh_cost_lifecycle.json", {"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
+             "port_closed": closed, "database_unchanged": before == after,
+             "before": before, "after": after, "exit": proc.returncode})
+        assert closed and before == after

## Limits, standards and reproduction

The UI harness executes the actual complete current page script in Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch stubs. It verifies generated markup, callbacks, request ordering and errors; it does not verify browser paint/layout or browser-executed exploit behavior. The extra 36-case CLI check verifies HTTP endpoint payloads, not browser rendering. No browser-executed XSS safety claim is made. The supplied database has no self-loop or unknown node type; unknown-type display uses an isolated synthetic UI fixture. Infinity yields controlled HTTP 400 and no graph value. No model was called by the exercised application paths; `Engine.ask` specifies `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). The application opens SQLite read/write (`src/app.py:41`), so observed read-only behavior is path/integrity evidence, not an enforced global connection permission.

**Standards axis: 0 documented violations.** No tracked standards files were found; the user-provided evidence and no-tracked-edit instructions govern. No application code change was applied, so the conditional KOA Studio / KhOpenApi original/new-code comparison is not triggered. [Standards review](standards_findings.md), [evidence](standards_evidence.json).

Run from repository root, with bytecode disabled. The exhaustive oracle is intentionally one pass in the runner. `ui_assertions.py` requires the root review's completed `root_checks/http_results.json` and `served_try.html`. Port 8904 must be available for the final command.

```text
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/db_ui/run_replays.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/db_ui/ui_assertions.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/db_ui/demo_http.py
```

**Scope conclusion:** previously repaired graph/database/UI findings remain fixed; demos have no observed regression; unchanged blank-submit and Infinity qualifications are separately retained. Overall approval depends on the primary report's refusal and cost investigations.

702:         # The class check runs on the surface *before* cleaning as well as after.
703:         # clean_surface strips "therapy" and "treatment" as regimen words, so
704:         # "Radiation therapy" arrives here as "Radiation" and could never reach
705:         # its own guard. An adversarial review found three CLASS_TERMS entries
706:         # that were dead code for exactly this reason.
707:         raw_part = raw_of.get(part, part) if len(parts) > 1 else raw
708:         if T.is_negated(raw_part) or T.is_negated(part):
709:             res["tier"] = TIER_BLOCKED_NEGATED
710:             res["rules"].append("negation_guard")
711:             res["note"] = "negated substance: the arm records its absence"
712:             out["components"].append(res)
713:             continue
714:         if (T.is_class_term(part) or T.is_class_term(raw_part)
715:                 or T.is_class_like(part) or T.is_class_like(raw_part)):
716:             res["tier"] = TIER_BLOCKED_CLASS
717:             res["rules"].append("class_term_guard")
718:             res["note"] = "category term, not an identifiable substance"
719:             out["components"].append(res)
720:             continue
721: 
722:         # A biosimilar, a differently-formulated product or a sponsor-specific
723:         # version is a *different* regulatory entity from the originator, even
724:         # though the molecule name matches. Resolving "Bevacizumab Biosimilar
725:         # HLX04" to BEVACIZUMAB asserts that Henlius sponsors trials of
726:         # Genentech's product and makes the actual asset disappear. The
727:         # vocabulary has no identifier for these, so the honest answer is a
728:         # refusal with a reason code, not the originator.
729:         if T.is_distinct_product(raw_part) or T.is_distinct_product(part):
730:             res["tier"] = TIER_BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT
731:             res["rules"].append("distinct_product_guard")
732:             res["note"] = ("distinct product (biosimilar or alternative "
733:                            "formulation); not the originator molecule and not "
734:                            "in this vocabulary")
735:             out["components"].append(res)
736:             continue
737: 
738:         # a radiolabelled tracer is its own entity; its unlabelled moiety is not
739:         # the administered drug, so no component of it may be matched
740:         if (T.is_radiotracer(part) or T.is_isotope_label(part)
741:                 or T.is_radiotracer(raw_part)):
742:             res["tier"] = TIER_BLOCKED_CLASS
743:             res["rules"].append("radiotracer_guard")

752:         hits = _lookup(index, part)
753:         if hits and T.RE_ESTER_FORM.search(part):
754:             hits = [h for h in hits if h["alias_type"] == "preferred_name"]
755:         tier = None
756:         if hits:

758:                     else TIER_ALIAS)
759: 
760:         # 1b. a target/gene symbol is not a drug ("PD-1", "EGFR")

762:             res["tier"] = TIER_BLOCKED_TARGET

766:             continue

780:                     res["component"] = p

100:     "adenovirus", "adenoviral vector", "recombinant adenovirus", "vaccinia",
101:     "vaccinia virus", "lentivirus", "lentiviral vector", "retrovirus",
102:     "retroviral vector", "plasmid", "plasmid dna", "mrna", "mrna vaccine",
103:     "aav", "adeno-associated virus", "viral vector", "virus",
104: }
105: 
106: # A category pattern, rather than an exact category string. A head noun like
107: # "inhibitor" or "blockade" qualified by a gene or pathway is a class, whatever
108: # the qualifier is, so the exact-membership set above can never be complete.
109: RE_CLASS_PATTERN = re.compile(
110:     r"^(?:[A-Za-z0-9\-/\s]{2,90}?)\s*"
111:     r"(?:inhibitors?|blockades?|antagonists?|agonists?|modulators?|"
112:     r"chemotherapy|chemotherapies|immunotherapy|immunotherapies|"
113:     r"radiotherapy|regimens?|doublets?|triplets?|backbones?|"
114:     r"antibod(?:y|ies)|conjugates?|vaccines?|inhibition)$",
115:     re.I,
116: )
117: 
118: # Words that make the pattern above a *product* rather than a class: a named
119: # molecule qualified by a form word is still that molecule. The stem has to
120: # end a word *with a real prefix in front of it*. Written as a whole-word
121: # match on the stem itself, this exempted only the literal word "mab": it let
122: # "mab inhibitor" through as a product and blocked "pembrolizumab antibody"
123: # as a class.
124: RE_CLASS_PATTERN_EXEMPT = re.compile(
125:     r"\b[a-z]{3,}(?:mab|nib|tinib|ciclib|parib|zomib|sertib|degib|rafenib)\b", re.I)
126: 
127: 
128: def is_class_like(text: str) -> bool:
129:     """True for a category description that no single molecule can satisfy."""
130:     t = (text or "").strip()
131:     if not t or RE_CLASS_PATTERN_EXEMPT.search(t):
132:         return False
133:     return bool(RE_CLASS_PATTERN.match(t))
134: 
135: 
136: # A biosimilar, a nanoparticle/liposomal/pegylated reformulation, or a
137: # sponsor-suffixed version is a separate regulatory product from the
138: # originator. The molecule name is the same; the asset is not.

368: # turn "CHOP" into prednisone and "GemCis" into cisplatin.
369: REGIMEN_ACRONYMS = {
370:     "chop", "rchop", "chope", "cvp", "folfox", "folfiri", "folfirinox",
371:     "xelox", "capox", "gemcis", "gemcarbo", "gemox", "abvd", "beacopp", "mvac",
372:     "ddmvac", "pfregimen", "tcbh", "tcb", "ecf", "eox", "flot", "icecure",
373:     "vac", "vip", "pcv", "tpf", "dcf", "gp", "gc", "tc", "cp", "pc", "ep",
374:     "ac", "ec", "fec", "cmf", "rd", "vrd", "vd", "mp", "dm", "ice", "rice",
375:     "mice", "epoch", "repoch", "dhap", "eshap", "gdp", "beam", "cav", "cvd",
376: }
377: 
378: 
379: def _same_entity_key(key: str) -> str:
380:     """The key a name lands on after same-entity merging."""
381:     for k, same in CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
382:         if key == k:
383:             return same
384:     return key
385: 
386: 
387: def _ambiguous_short_alias(alias: str, index: dict) -> bool:
388:     """A short registry alias is kept only when it is a three-letter
389:     abbreviation that the whole index binds to one molecule ("ptx", "doc").
390:     Two letters or fewer never identify a substance."""
391:     key = T.canonical_key(alias)
392:     if len(key) <= 2:
393:         return True
394:     return len({e["drug_id"] for e in index.get(key, [])}) != 1
395: 
396: 
397: def _foreign_registry_alias(alias: str, drug: dict) -> str | None:
398:     """Why a registry-derived synonym cannot stand for this molecule, or None.
399: 
400:     The ingredient test is deliberately narrow. A first version flagged every
401:     INN-shaped word that did not share a stem with the molecule's name, and so
402:     removed former names and misspellings that are correct: apatinib under
403:     RIVOCERANIB (the same molecule renamed), hydroxydaunomycin under
404:     DOXORUBICIN, interleukin-2 under ALDESLEUKIN, "triplizumab" under
405:     TORIPALIMAB. A word is kept when the molecule's own curated (ChEMBL)
406:     synonyms contain it, or when it is a near-spelling of the molecule's name.
407:     """
408:     if T.is_distinct_product(alias) or T.canonical_key(alias) in DISTINCT_PRODUCT_ALIASES:
409:         return "distinct_product"
410:     if T.canonical_key(alias) in REGIMEN_ACRONYMS:
411:         return "regimen"
412:     name_key = T.canonical_key(drug["name"])
413:     curated_text = " ".join(a["alias"].lower() for a in drug["aliases"]
414:                             if a.get("source") != "AACT")
415:     curated_words = set(re.findall(r"[a-z]{5,}", curated_text)) | {name_key}
416:     words = re.findall(r"[a-z]+", alias.lower())
417:     # a multi-word name that contains the molecule's own name is that
418:     # molecule's full name ("nogapendekin alfa inbakicept"), not a foreign one
419:     if any(w[:5] in name_key for w in words if len(w) >= 5):
420:         return None
421:     for w in words:
422:         if not RE_INN_SHAPE.match(w):
423:             continue
424:         if name_key[:5] in w or w in curated_text:
425:             continue
426:         # a misspelling of the name or of a curated synonym ("anrotinib")
427:         if max(fuzz.ratio(w, c) for c in curated_words) >= 80:
428:             continue
429:         return "different_ingredient"
430:     return None
431: 
432: 
433: def detect_duplicate_entities(dic: dict) -> list[dict]:
434:     """Two identifiers that appear to describe one substance.
435: 
436:     Reported, never auto-merged: merging reference identifiers on the strength of
437:     name overlap alone would destroy information that a curator may need.
438:     """
439:     drugs = dic["drugs"]
440:     by_alias: dict[str, set] = defaultdict(set)

## Blocking finding A: whole-name fast path overrides an established refusal

Exact POST `/api/explore` payload:

```json
{"text":"bevacizumab-awwb?"}
```

HTTP 200 returns `center.id="CHEMBL1201583"`, `label="BEVACIZUMAB"`, `trials=122`, `matched_text="Bevacizumab awwb"`, and `also=[]`. The entire input names a qualified product, not an unqualified originator plus a separate accepted mention.

| Path / control | Observed result |
|---|---|
| `resolve_drug_string` through `/api/resolve` | `ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583`; no refusal |
| `_resolver_blocked("bevacizumab-awwb?")` | `{}` |
| `_link("bevacizumab-awwb?")` | only `Refused`, label `bevacizumab-awwb` |
| `_whole_name` / public `link_entities` | `Drug / CHEMBL1201583` |
| Exact `b7a00f9` linker map | refused `bevacizumab-awwb` |
| Current bare `bevacizumab-awwb` | refused |
| Current `bevacizumab-awwb!` | same originator leak; previous linker refused |
| Current `"bevacizumab-awwb"` including literal quotes | same originator leak even though current `_link` refuses; previous linker also leaked this quoted form |

**This is new versus `b7a00f9` for `?` and `!`.** Do not generalize that status to every wrapper variant.

Cause: `src/query.py:138-142` returns `_whole_name` before `_link`. `_whole_name` derives its lookup key from punctuation-stripped text (`191-194`) but checks `is_distinct_product` on the original punctuated text (`195-196`). The anchored suffix regex (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213`) fails on the punctuation. Aggressive canonicalization (`339-346`) nevertheless hits the stored originator alias. By contrast, `_link` removes the edge punctuation before checking its tokens (`src/query.py:217-220,252-264`) and correctly refuses this exact input; that result is never consulted. `Engine.explore` trusts the public link at `src/app.py:88-95`.

The precise QA control `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb?` has **zero evidence**, and `Which trials evaluate "bevacizumab-awwb"?` also has zero. Adding a question prefix prevents the whole-input alias hit and reaches the working item linker. This is a **map fast-path** blocker, not a claim that this exact punctuated QA sentence leaks.

Required outcome: whole-name recognition must preserve the refusal checks on the normalized text that enables the positive match. A genuine stored-name exception must not turn a correctly refused suffix into a drug merely because punctuation made the raw anchored guard miss it.

## Blocking finding B: shared refusal predicates accept equivalent distinct-product spellings

These cases fulfill the specifically requested attack: **both** the resolver and the independent list-item linker miss the refusal.

| Exact input | Resolver | `_link` and map | Control |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(bevacizumab-awwb)` | `ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583` | BEVACIZUMAB, 122 trials, no refusal | bare `bevacizumab-awwb` refuses |
| `[bevacizumab-awwb]` | same | same | bare suffix refuses |
| `bevacizumab‑awwb` with U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN | same | same | ASCII-hyphen suffix refuses |
| `bio‑similar bevacizumab` with U+2011 | `CONTAINED_ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583` plus unresolved qualifier | same | `bio-similar bevacizumab` refuses |

U+2010 and U+2014 substitutions also leak in the saved matrix. All named rows above have actual HTTP explore/resolve confirmation except the U+2010/U+2014 supplementary variants, which are in-process results.

QA impact, confirmed over HTTP:

| Exact question | Originator evidence |
|---|---|
| `Which trials evaluate (bevacizumab-awwb)?` | 12 rows, first `NCT00955305\|EVALUATES\|CHEMBL1201583\|NCT00955305` |
| `Which companies sponsor (bevacizumab-awwb)?` | 12 rows, first `ORG:roche\|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF\|CHEMBL1201583\|` |
| `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab‑awwb?` (U+2011) | 12 originator trial rows |
| `Which trials evaluate bio‑similar bevacizumab?` (U+2011) | 12 originator trial rows |

Cause: `_link` deliberately retains parentheses/brackets at `src/query.py:217-220`; the anchored biosimilar suffix guard (`src/textrules.py:156-157`) does not tolerate those wrappers. The suffix pattern permits ASCII hyphen and en dash only; the `bio-similar` qualifier pattern permits ASCII hyphen only (`139-140`). Meanwhile `canonical_key` drops or normalizes these distinctions (`339-346`), enabling aliases the guard did not recognize. The resolver uses the same product predicate at `src/normalize.py:729-735`, so running it first cannot repair this shared blind spot. `_qualified` at `src/query.py:99-110` merely retries the same predicates over word runs.

These are **inherited**, confirmed with `b7a00f9`; they are not evidence that the latest repair introduced every refusal defect. They remain approval blockers because wrapping a refused product name, or replacing its hyphen with a normal Unicode hyphen, does not name the originator instead. An extra accepted occurrence of the originator is absent from every primary reproduction.

## Blocking finding C: a space-separated isotope prefix passes both gates

Exact input **`89Zr trastuzumab`** returns HTTP 200 with **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, **13 trials**, `also=[]`. The resolver returns `ALIAS` on stored synonym **`89zr-trastuzumab`**, and `_link` independently returns the same originator. `_resolver_blocked` is empty. Both **`89Zr-trastuzumab`** and **`[89Zr] trastuzumab`** refuse over HTTP.

`Which trials evaluate 89Zr trastuzumab?` returns **12** originator rows; first `NCT00016367|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201585|NCT00016367`, whose stored intervention is `Trastuzumab`. The bracketed QA control returns zero evidence. `89Zr‑trastuzumab` with U+2011 also passes both gates in-process.

Cause: `src/textrules.py:303-305` requires either a hyphen after an unbracketed isotope or brackets around the isotope. A space-only prefix satisfies neither alternative. The isotope label predicate separately recognizes `89Zr` (`289-298`) but the resolver calls it on the complete component (`src/normalize.py:740-741`), not the isotope token; `_qualified` uses `is_radiotracer`, not `is_isotope_label` (`src/query.py:105-110`). Canonical matching then reaches the already stored radiolabel synonym (`src/textrules.py:339-346`).

This is **inherited**, confirmed using `b7a00f9`. The repository's own alias and rejected one-character spelling controls establish the tracer premise; this finding does not assume an external clinical relationship.

## Separate should-fix: canonical-key equality is broader than exactly one stored name

These are current regressions versus `b7a00f9`, not extra refused-product blockers:

| Exact input | Current map | `_link` and prior map | Stored alias actually matched |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Erlotinib, hydrochloride` | ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742, 31 trials | ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553, 118 trials | `ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE` |
| `osimertinib,mesylate` | OSIMERTINIB MESYLATE / CHEMBL3545063, 7 trials | OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410, 79 trials | `OSIMERTINIB MESYLATE` |
| `is a` | ISATUXIMAB / CHEMBL3545131, 1 trial | no entity / not_found | `isa` |

All three rows have actual HTTP confirmation. The comma inputs have no exact case-insensitive stored-alias match; [dictionary evidence](dictionary_evidence.json) records every index hit. Full resolver requests retain the parent as one component and leave `hydrochloride`/`mesylate` unresolved. The map's salt selection therefore comes from the whole-name exception, which erases the declared item boundary. The list linker itself stays within the boundary. Slash/pipe and `and` controls preserve the parent. Semicolon and all three declared CJK comma/semicolon variants repeat the salt regression in-process.

`_whole_name` at `src/query.py:189-195` excludes slash/pipe and listing words, but accepts every string with an equivalent aggressive key. It does not compare to the stored name's actual punctuation/token boundaries. This also bypasses the explicit `is a` safeguard at `src/query.py:302-307`. Genuine stored-punctuation controls `CP-358,774` and `Rapamycin, 42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-` have exact stored alias matches and retain their intended complete identities; preserving those does not require joining arbitrary new comma lists.

The corresponding prefixed QA inputs `Which trials evaluate Erlotinib, hydrochloride?` and `Which trials evaluate osimertinib,mesylate?` correctly return parent-drug evidence. The current mismatch is in selecting the map centre, not a claim that the salt's displayed count differs from its stored value.

## Controls and scope limits

* Mixed separately accepted aliases (`biosimilar anastrozole, Arimidex`, slash variant, `Mvasi and bevacizumab`, `bevacizumab-awwb, bevacizumab`) retain their independently named accepted drug. Whole-item over-refusal is not used as a failure criterion.
* `CP-358,774`, the complete rapamycin alias, and the long paclitaxel-poliglumex alias were exercised as exact-name controls. Known `Pegylated interferon alfa-2a` over-refusal is retained and not newly escalated.
* Qualifier carry with both punctuation and conjunction (`biosimilar, and osimertinib`, etc.) is recorded diagnostically. It is not promoted to a blocker because the requested carry rule could intentionally interpret the conjunction across that punctuation. The unambiguous fast-path and shared-refusal counterexamples are sufficient.
* No assertion is made that adding more calls to the existing resolver can repair findings B/C: both paths demonstrably share the failing predicates. No application changes were applied.

+def post(path, payload, timeout=20):
+    start = time.perf_counter()
+    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),

+    try:
+        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout)
+    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:

+            "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "bytes": len(raw), "body": json.loads(raw)}

- All **13 inherited m5/m6 name scripts** exited 0: [execution](replay_execution.json), [provenance](replay_manifest.json). Their algorithm/input logic was left unchanged; only output destinations moved. This includes **62 original inputs + 14 extra probes**, **30 m3 boundary cases + 21 joiner aliases**, **45 m4 attacks + 37 QA controls + 2,584 multi-token aliases**, and **113 m5 attacks + 7,511 unique alias keys**, 49 confirmation cases, and 13 cross-item controls.

Only their output paths and original fixed port changed. The unchanged CLI served actual HTTP on owned port **8903**, including the extra-script HTTP list. Those runs are retained but **not interpreted as no-relink evidence**. ## Refusal gaps found in the replay

`(bevacizumab-awwb)` and `[bevacizumab-awwb]` genuinely pass **both** refusal layers: resolver accepts ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583, `_resolver_blocked` returns `{}`, `_link` returns BEVACIZUMAB, and `explore` draws that originator. Trial and sponsor questions each return **12** originator evidence rows. Bare `bevacizumab-awwb` refuses. The punctuation wraps the same refused suffix; no external pharmacology premise is required. These were already present in the m6 fresh attack and remain **NOT FIXED**, although the narrower named M6-B2 examples are fixed. Current guards preserve brackets (`src/query.py:218-220`), the suffix pattern is anchored (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213-223`), and later canonical alias matching removes the wrapper. [Stage-isolated result](m7_focus.json), [exact reused m6 attack](fresh_results.json).

`bevacizumab-awwb?` is an additional **new fast-path regression**: the prior 514929c linker refuses, current map draws BEVACIZUMAB. The resolver accepts it; `_whole_name` obtains the positive key using `text.strip(" ?.!")` (`src/query.py:191`), but tests the raw punctuated input for refusal (`195-196`), then returns before `_link` (`138-142`). `_link` alone correctly refuses this input. Quoted suffix/tracer forms have the same fast-path-versus-list-linker disagreement. Do not incorrectly classify these latter cases as both guards missing. [Comparison](fresh_results.json), [stages](m7_focus.json). Parent review supplies independent CLI HTTP confirmation.

The non-parenthetical `doxorubicin in nanoparticle and liposomal formulations` still maps doxorubicin in the reused reversed probe. Under the explicit current listing-word policy this has separated items, so this report does not promote it beyond the demonstrated bracketed suffix blockers.

## Should-fix: full-name shortcut excludes stored slash and listing-word names

Of **7,511** unique alias keys, the inherited before/after sweep finds **20** formerly linked aliases with no current Drug: the accepted M5-S2 plus **19** slash/joiner-containing aliases. No alias in this sweep now returns a *different* drug ID from the unique stored ID. The complete lost-alias list and stored records are in [names_m5_summary.json](names_m5_summary.json).

Actual unchanged-server HTTP on owned port 8903 confirms:

| Exact input | Stored unique Drug | Resolver | Map |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(E/Z)-AFATINIB` | CHEMBL1173655 | EXACT / same ID | HTTP 200 not_found |
| `RO 09-1978/000` | CHEMBL1773 | ALIAS / same ID | HTTP 200 not_found |
| `Anusol plus hc` | CHEMBL389621 | UNRESOLVED + BLOCKED_TARGET_TERM; neither a distinct-product nor tracer refusal | HTTP 200 not_found |

The corresponding exact IDs draw live nodes. [HTTP, stored-index proof, and ID controls](alias_http.json), [server lifecycle](alias_lifecycle.json). `_whole_name` rejects any slash/pipe or standalone joiner **before** checking whether the whole input uniquely names a stored drug (`src/query.py:189-194`). This contradicts the stated whole-name precedence for a single stored, non-refused input. It is a lone-valid-name should-fix, separate from originator leakage and from M5-S2.

+## Blocking findings
+
+### M7-B1 — P1: whole-name recognition overrides a refusal already made correctly by `_link`
+
+Exact request:
+
+```json
+{"text":"bevacizumab-awwb?"}
+```
+
+`POST /api/explore` returns **HTTP 200**, **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, `matched_text:"Bevacizumab awwb"`, and `also:[]`. There is no separate accepted originator mention.
+
+| Stage/control | Current result |
+|---|---|
+| `/api/resolve` | `ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583`, no refusal |
+| `_resolver_blocked` | `{}` |
+| `_link` alone | only `Refused`, label `bevacizumab-awwb` |
+| `_whole_name`, then public `link_entities` | Drug / CHEMBL1201583 |
+| Bare `bevacizumab-awwb` | refused |
+| Exact `b7a00f9` linker on `bevacizumab-awwb?` | refused |
+
+**New versus the reviewed m6 HEAD.** `bevacizumab-awwb!` also regresses. Literal quoted `"bevacizumab-awwb"` demonstrates the same current shortcut bypass, although that quoted spelling also leaked in the predecessor. [Actual HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [current/previous stages](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent historical replay](names/fresh_results.json).
+
+**Cause:** `src/query.py:138-142` returns `_whole_name` before consulting `_link`. The shortcut creates its key from punctuation-stripped text (`191-194`) but runs product/tracer guards on the original text (`195-196`). The anchored suffix pattern (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213`) misses the question mark; `canonical_key` (`339-346`) still obtains the originator alias. `_link` would remove that punctuation and refuse (`src/query.py:217-220,252-264`). `Engine.explore` trusts the shortcut's hit (`src/app.py:88-95`).
+
+The exact prefixed QA control `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb?` returns **zero evidence**: its prefix prevents the whole-input shortcut and reaches the working item linker. This is a map-centre regression, not a claim that that precise QA sentence leaks.
+
+**Required outcome:** a whole-name exception must preserve refusal on the representation that enables its positive match. It must not override the correct refusal merely because punctuation defeats a raw anchored predicate.
+
+### M7-B2 — P1: product wrappers and Unicode hyphens defeat both guards
+
+These satisfy the requested **both-paths-miss** attack:
+
+| Exact input | Resolver | `_link` and actual map |
+|---|---|---|
+| `(bevacizumab-awwb)` | ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583 | BEVACIZUMAB, 122 trials; no refusal |
+| `[bevacizumab-awwb]` | same | same |
+| `bevacizumab‑awwb` — internal U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN | same | same |
+| `bio‑similar bevacizumab` — U+2011 | CONTAINED_ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583 plus unresolved qualifier | same |
+
+Bare ASCII `bevacizumab-awwb` and `bio-similar bevacizumab` refuse. Every row above has actual HTTP explore/resolve confirmation and direct `_link` evidence; no exception for a separately accepted molecule applies. [HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [stages and stored aliases](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent replay](names/m7_focus.json).
+
+`Which trials evaluate (bevacizumab-awwb)?` returns **12 originator rows**, first `NCT00955305|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201583|NCT00955305`. `Which companies sponsor (bevacizumab-awwb)?` returns **12**, first `ORG:roche|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL1201583|`. The U+2011 suffix and `bio‑similar` trial questions also return 12 originator rows over HTTP.
+
+**Cause:** `_link` retains brackets (`src/query.py:217-220`), while the biosimilar suffix predicate anchors the whole input and accepts only ASCII hyphen/en dash (`src/textrules.py:156-157`). The `bio-similar` pattern permits only ASCII hyphen (`139-140`). The later canonical key removes/normalizes these distinctions (`339-346`). The resolver calls the same product predicate (`src/normalize.py:729-735`), so running it first provides no independent protection. `_qualified` simply retries these predicates (`src/query.py:99-110`).
+
+These are **inherited**, confirmed against the exact prior linker. The parenthesized/bracketed probes already appeared in m6's supplementary attack and remain **NOT FIXED**, despite repair of m6's named U+3002 example. The repository's own refused ASCII controls establish the product premise; no external pharmacology assumption is needed.
+
+**Required outcome:** equivalent wrappers/hyphen spellings of the refused product must not unlock its originator alias, in either map or QA.
+
+### M7-B3 — P1: a space-separated isotope prefix passes both guards
+
+**`89Zr trastuzumab`** returns HTTP 200, **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, **13 trials**, `also:[]`. The resolver returns **ALIAS** using stored synonym **`89zr-trastuzumab`**; `_link` returns the same originator and `_resolver_blocked` is empty. Both `89Zr-trastuzumab` and `[89Zr] trastuzumab` refuse over HTTP. [HTTP/stages](attacks/REPORT.md#blocking-finding-c-a-space-separated-isotope-prefix-passes-both-gates), [stored alias](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).
+
+`Which trials evaluate 89Zr trastuzumab?` returns **12 originator rows**, first `NCT00016367|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201585|NCT00016367`, with stored intervention `Trastuzumab`. The bracketed QA control returns zero.
+
+**Cause:** `src/textrules.py:303-305` requires a hyphen after an unbracketed isotope, or brackets around it. `is_isotope_label` recognizes the separate `89Zr` token (`289-298`), but the resolver tests the entire component (`src/normalize.py:740-741`), and `_qualified` never calls that token predicate (`src/query.py:105-110`). Canonical alias lookup then accepts the stored radiolabel synonym. This is **inherited**, not introduced by HEAD.
+
+**Required outcome:** the isotope-bearing name must remain refused when the isotope and moiety are separated by whitespace; neither map nor QA should substitute the unlabelled originator.
+

+## Should-fix findings, separate from approval blockers
+
+### M7-S1 — P2: canonical equality is broader than exactly one stored name
+
+| Exact input | Current map | `_link` / exact prior linker |
+|---|---|---|
+| `Erlotinib, hydrochloride` | ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742, 31 trials | ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553, 118 trials |
+| `osimertinib,mesylate` | OSIMERTINIB MESYLATE / CHEMBL3545063, 7 trials | OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410, 79 trials |
+| `is a` | ISATUXIMAB / CHEMBL3545131, 1 trial | no entity / not_found |
+
+All three are actual HTTP reproductions and **new versus b7a00f9**. None exactly matches a stored alias, even case-insensitively; they only match the aggressive key. The comma inputs violate the declared list boundary. The ordinary-word input bypasses the explicit `is a` safeguard in `_link`. [HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [dictionary proof](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).
+
+`_whole_name` (`src/query.py:189-195`) erases punctuation and whitespace via `canonical_key` and never checks the stored name's actual token/punctuation boundaries; `_link` correctly enforces boundaries and the single-token abbreviation safeguard (`293-307`). Genuine complete-name controls `CP-358,774` and `Rapamycin, 42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-` have stored full-name matches and correctly retain their intended identities. Their exception need not accept arbitrary new comma lists. Prefixed salt QA stays on the parent drug; the salt counts themselves are accurately stored values.
+
+### M7-S2 — P2: exact stored names are rejected before whole-name lookup
+
+**`(E/Z)-AFATINIB`** uniquely names **CHEMBL1173655**, and `/api/resolve` returns **EXACT**. **`RO 09-1978/000`** uniquely names **CHEMBL1773**, with resolver **ALIAS**. Both `/api/explore` requests return HTTP 200 **not_found**, while the exact IDs draw live nodes. [Actual HTTP, stored-index proof and ID controls](names/alias_http.json).
+
+`src/query.py:189-190` rejects slash/pipe or a listing word before checking whether the input is exactly one stored non-refused name. Thus step (2)'s intended precedence does not cover these valid lone names. The sweep has 20 formerly recognized names with no current Drug, including known M5-S2; it has **zero remaining wrong-ID cases among the 7,511 unique stored keys**. Some lost-alias resolver statuses differ, so the two positively resolved controls above are the strongest should-fix proof. [Complete list](names/names_m5_summary.json). No demo is lost.
+
+### M7-S3 — P2: per-piece full resolution causes new multi-second lock contention
+
+The old cubic all-substring defect remains repaired. The new resolver call for **every list piece**, including identical repeated pieces (`src/query.py:159-164`), introduces a different cost. A same-data, three-sample comparison loads the exact **b7a00f9 query module** and current query module without changing shared rules/data. A second measurement ran after all other audit workers stopped; this isolates audit workloads, not unrelated host activity.
+
+| Exact linker input | Characters | b7a00f9 median | Current median |
+|---|---:|---:|---:|
+| `" ".join(["x"]*250)` | 499 | 0.0219 s | 0.0298 s |
+| `";".join(["x"]*250)` | 499 | 0.0057 s | 0.2537 s |
+| `";".join(["x"]*1000)` | 1,999 | 0.0512 s | 1.3120 s |
+| `";".join(["x"]*2000)` | 3,999 | 0.1779 s | 2.5561 s |
+
+The 2,000-piece stage check spends **2.5653 s** in `_resolver_blocked`, versus **0.2734 s** in `_link`. Measurements are separate calls and need not sum to a median. [Samples/stage timings](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_comparison.json), [reproducer](root_checks/fresh_cost.py).
+
+Actual unchanged CLI HTTP, within existing caps:
+
+- 499-character semicolon text: `/api/explore` **0.3160 s**, `/api/ask` **0.3148 s**, both 200.
+- `POST /api/check` with `{"text":"EGFR","answer":";".join(["x"]*2000)}`: **7.7280 s**, HTTP 200.
+- Ordinary EGFR map dispatched **0.1010 s** after that check: **7.6665 s**, HTTP 200. EGFR before/after: **0.0384 / 0.0412 s**. GET `/` remains responsive.
+- First independent run also reproduced the delay: check **6.0783 s**, queued EGFR **6.0118 s**. No timeout/deadlock inference is needed; these responses completed.
+
+[Isolated HTTP](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_http.json), [first HTTP run](root_checks/fresh_cost_http.json), [owned-server cleanup](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_lifecycle.json). The inherited m6 stress script independently records split_2000 at **3.4737 s** median linking / **7.0111 s** `Engine.check`; plain 2,000-token input is far cheaper. [Exact inherited stress](root_checks/cost_stress.json).
+
+The application holds `engine.lock` across linking/checking (`src/app.py:247-263`), and the answer cap is 4,000 characters (`260`). Avoid repeating full resolver work for identical pieces and bound total work across every caller, while preserving the refusal semantics. This is a should-fix cost regression, separate from the originator-leak approval blockers. These timings are local observations, not universal latency guarantees or a claim that the entire algorithm has one asymptotic complexity.
+

+## Reproduction
+
+Run from the repository root with bytecode disabled. Copies/manifests above preserve original-script provenance; HTTP scripts sharing a port must run sequentially. Worker reports include their individual commands. The isolated benchmark is the only repeated cost run, used to remove concurrent audit workload as a confounder.
+
+```text
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/run_verification.py
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/run_replays.py http
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/run_m6_http.py m6_http
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/run_replays.py perf
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/run_perf_current.py
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/cost_stress.py
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/root_checks/fresh_cost.py isolated
+python -B audit_evidence_claims/m7/names/run_replays.py

+"""Bounded, same-data cost comparison and owned CLI lock-contention check."""
+import concurrent.futures
+import hashlib
+import json
+import os
+from pathlib import Path
+import socket
+import statistics
+import subprocess
+import sys
+import time
+import types
+import urllib.error
+import urllib.request
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
+PORT = 8902
+BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
+PREFIX = "isolated_" if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == "isolated" else ""
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
+
+
+def dump(name, obj):
+    (OUT / (PREFIX + name)).write_text(json.dumps(obj, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf8")
+
+
+if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == "worker":
+    import query
+    ref, kind, size = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], int(sys.argv[4])
+    mod = query
+    if ref != "HEAD":
+        mod = types.ModuleType("cost_previous")
+        mod.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
+        sys.modules[mod.__name__] = mod
+        code = subprocess.check_output(["git", "show", f"{ref}:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT).decode("utf8")
+        exec(compile(code, mod.__file__, "exec"), mod.__dict__)
+    kg = mod.OncoKG()
+    kg.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
+    value = {"plain": " ", "split": ";", "slash": "/"}[kind].join(["x"] * size)
+    times = []
+    for _ in range(3):
+        start = time.perf_counter()
+        entities = kg.link_entities(value)
+        times.append(time.perf_counter() - start)
+    detail = {}
+    if ref == "HEAD":
+        for name in ["_resolver_blocked", "_link"]:
+            start = time.perf_counter()
+            result = getattr(kg, name)(value)
+            detail[name] = {"seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "result_count": len(result)}
+    kg.conn.close()
+    print(json.dumps({"ref": ref, "kind": kind, "size": size, "characters": len(value),
+                      "times": times, "median": statistics.median(times),
+                      "entities": entities, "stages": detail}))
+    raise SystemExit(0)
+
+
+def post(path, payload, timeout=20):
+    start = time.perf_counter()
+    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
+                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
+    try:
+        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout)
+    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
+        response = error
+    raw = response.read()
+    return {"path": path, "payload": payload, "status": response.status,
+            "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "bytes": len(raw), "body": json.loads(raw)}
+
+
+env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
+       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TEMP": str(OUT / "tmp"),
+       "TMP": str(OUT / "tmp"), "TMPDIR": str(OUT / "tmp")}
+rows = []
+for kind, size in [("plain", 250), ("split", 250), ("split", 1000), ("split", 2000)]:
+    for ref in ["b7a00f9", "HEAD"]:
+        try:
+            result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", __file__, "worker", ref, kind, str(size)],
+                                    cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, timeout=35)
+            assert result.returncode == 0, result.stderr.decode("utf8", "replace")
+            row = json.loads(result.stdout)
+        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
+            row = {"ref": ref, "kind": kind, "size": size, "timeout_seconds": 35}
+        rows.append(row)
+        dump("fresh_cost_comparison.json", rows)
+        print(json.dumps(row), flush=True)
+
+db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
+before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+with socket.socket() as sock:
+    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port occupied"
+with (OUT / (PREFIX + "fresh_cost_server.txt")).open("w", encoding="utf8") as log:
+    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
+                            env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
+                            creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
+    try:
+        for _ in range(100):
+            assert proc.poll() is None
+            try:
+                urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
+                break
+            except OSError:
+                time.sleep(.1)
+        else:
+            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
+        baseline = post("/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+        value = ";".join(["x"] * 250)
+        sequential = [post(path, {"text": value}) for path in ["/api/explore", "/api/ask"]]
+        answer = ";".join(["x"] * 2000)
+        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
+            started = time.perf_counter()
+            leader = pool.submit(post, "/api/check", {"text": "EGFR", "answer": answer})
+            time.sleep(.1)
+            dispatched = time.perf_counter() - started
+            follower = pool.submit(post, "/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+            get_start = time.perf_counter()
+            response = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=3)
+            response.read()
+            live_get = {"status": response.status, "seconds": time.perf_counter() - get_start}
+            check, queued = leader.result(), follower.result()
+        after_request = post("/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+        dump("fresh_cost_http.json", {"baseline": baseline, "sequential": sequential,
+             "check": check, "queued_egfr": queued, "follower_dispatched_seconds": dispatched,
+             "live_get": live_get, "normal_after": after_request})
+        print(json.dumps({"check_seconds": check["seconds"], "queued_egfr_seconds": queued["seconds"],
+                          "normal_after_seconds": after_request["seconds"]}), flush=True)
+    finally:
+        proc.terminate()
+        proc.wait(timeout=10)
+        with socket.socket() as sock:
+            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
+        after = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+        dump("fresh_cost_lifecycle.json", {"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
+             "port_closed": closed, "database_unchanged": before == after,
+             "before": before, "after": after, "exit": proc.returncode})
+        assert closed and before == after

apply patch

+# Independent relation-map review — round 7
+
+Reviewed HEAD **`9bd7cb4b5d9270bbe99cb5dfec3993502f6b5377`**, 2026-11-03 KST. **Approval remains blocked.** The exact named m1–m6 originator-leak reproductions are repaired, but the new whole-name shortcut bypasses a correct item-linker refusal. Independently, product and tracer spellings still evade **both** refusal paths. The graph, stored-value and demo checks pass; they do not validate the identity selected from a refused product.
+
+No tracked/application file was edited. All new audit files are under `audit_evidence_claims/m7/`. The specified whole-item fail-closed behavior and known M5-S2 are accepted. Whole-input stored-name precedence is evaluated separately from ordinary list parsing.
+

+## Blocking findings
+
+### M7-B1 — P1: whole-name recognition overrides a refusal already made correctly by `_link`
+
+Exact request:
+
+```json
+{"text":"bevacizumab-awwb?"}
+```
+
+`POST /api/explore` returns **HTTP 200**, **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, `matched_text:"Bevacizumab awwb"`, and `also:[]`. There is no separate accepted originator mention.
+
+| Stage/control | Current result |
+|---|---|
+| `/api/resolve` | `ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583`, no refusal |
+| `_resolver_blocked` | `{}` |
+| `_link` alone | only `Refused`, label `bevacizumab-awwb` |
+| `_whole_name`, then public `link_entities` | Drug / CHEMBL1201583 |
+| Bare `bevacizumab-awwb` | refused |
+| Exact `b7a00f9` linker on `bevacizumab-awwb?` | refused |
+
+**New versus the reviewed m6 HEAD.** `bevacizumab-awwb!` also regresses. Literal quoted `"bevacizumab-awwb"` demonstrates the same current shortcut bypass, although that quoted spelling also leaked in the predecessor. [Actual HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [current/previous stages](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent historical replay](names/fresh_results.json).
+
+**Cause:** `src/query.py:138-142` returns `_whole_name` before consulting `_link`. The shortcut creates its key from punctuation-stripped text (`191-194`) but runs product/tracer guards on the original text (`195-196`). The anchored suffix pattern (`src/textrules.py:156-157,213`) misses the question mark; `canonical_key` (`339-346`) still obtains the originator alias. `_link` would remove that punctuation and refuse (`src/query.py:217-220,252-264`). `Engine.explore` trusts the shortcut's hit (`src/app.py:88-95`).
+
+The exact prefixed QA control `Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-awwb?` returns **zero evidence**: its prefix prevents the whole-input shortcut and reaches the working item linker. This is a map-centre regression, not a claim that that precise QA sentence leaks.
+
+**Required outcome:** a whole-name exception must preserve refusal on the representation that enables its positive match. It must not override the correct refusal merely because punctuation defeats a raw anchored predicate.
+
+### M7-B2 — P1: product wrappers and Unicode hyphens defeat both guards
+
+These satisfy the requested **both-paths-miss** attack:
+
+| Exact input | Resolver | `_link` and actual map |
+|---|---|---|
+| `(bevacizumab-awwb)` | ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583 | BEVACIZUMAB, 122 trials; no refusal |
+| `[bevacizumab-awwb]` | same | same |
+| `bevacizumab‑awwb` — internal U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN | same | same |
+| `bio‑similar bevacizumab` — U+2011 | CONTAINED_ALIAS / CHEMBL1201583 plus unresolved qualifier | same |
+
+Bare ASCII `bevacizumab-awwb` and `bio-similar bevacizumab` refuse. Every row above has actual HTTP explore/resolve confirmation and direct `_link` evidence; no exception for a separately accepted molecule applies. [HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [stages and stored aliases](attacks/fresh_results.json), [independent replay](names/m7_focus.json).
+
+`Which trials evaluate (bevacizumab-awwb)?` returns **12 originator rows**, first `NCT00955305|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201583|NCT00955305`. `Which companies sponsor (bevacizumab-awwb)?` returns **12**, first `ORG:roche|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL1201583|`. The U+2011 suffix and `bio‑similar` trial questions also return 12 originator rows over HTTP.
+
+**Cause:** `_link` retains brackets (`src/query.py:217-220`), while the biosimilar suffix predicate anchors the whole input and accepts only ASCII hyphen/en dash (`src/textrules.py:156-157`). The `bio-similar` pattern permits only ASCII hyphen (`139-140`). The later canonical key removes/normalizes these distinctions (`339-346`). The resolver calls the same product predicate (`src/normalize.py:729-735`), so running it first provides no independent protection. `_qualified` simply retries these predicates (`src/query.py:99-110`).
+
+These are **inherited**, confirmed against the exact prior linker. The parenthesized/bracketed probes already appeared in m6's supplementary attack and remain **NOT FIXED**, despite repair of m6's named U+3002 example. The repository's own refused ASCII controls establish the product premise; no external pharmacology assumption is needed.
+
+**Required outcome:** equivalent wrappers/hyphen spellings of the refused product must not unlock its originator alias, in either map or QA.
+
+### M7-B3 — P1: a space-separated isotope prefix passes both guards
+
+**`89Zr trastuzumab`** returns HTTP 200, **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, **13 trials**, `also:[]`. The resolver returns **ALIAS** using stored synonym **`89zr-trastuzumab`**; `_link` returns the same originator and `_resolver_blocked` is empty. Both `89Zr-trastuzumab` and `[89Zr] trastuzumab` refuse over HTTP. [HTTP/stages](attacks/REPORT.md#blocking-finding-c-a-space-separated-isotope-prefix-passes-both-gates), [stored alias](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).
+
+`Which trials evaluate 89Zr trastuzumab?` returns **12 originator rows**, first `NCT00016367|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201585|NCT00016367`, with stored intervention `Trastuzumab`. The bracketed QA control returns zero.
+
+**Cause:** `src/textrules.py:303-305` requires a hyphen after an unbracketed isotope, or brackets around it. `is_isotope_label` recognizes the separate `89Zr` token (`289-298`), but the resolver tests the entire component (`src/normalize.py:740-741`), and `_qualified` never calls that token predicate (`src/query.py:105-110`). Canonical alias lookup then accepts the stored radiolabel synonym. This is **inherited**, not introduced by HEAD.
+
+**Required outcome:** the isotope-bearing name must remain refused when the isotope and moiety are separated by whitespace; neither map nor QA should substitute the unlabelled originator.

+
+## Should-fix findings, separate from approval blockers
+
+### M7-S1 — P2: canonical equality is broader than exactly one stored name
+
+| Exact input | Current map | `_link` / exact prior linker |
+|---|---|---|
+| `Erlotinib, hydrochloride` | ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE / CHEMBL1079742, 31 trials | ERLOTINIB / CHEMBL553, 118 trials |
+| `osimertinib,mesylate` | OSIMERTINIB MESYLATE / CHEMBL3545063, 7 trials | OSIMERTINIB / CHEMBL3353410, 79 trials |
+| `is a` | ISATUXIMAB / CHEMBL3545131, 1 trial | no entity / not_found |
+
+All three are actual HTTP reproductions and **new versus b7a00f9**. None exactly matches a stored alias, even case-insensitively; they only match the aggressive key. The comma inputs violate the declared list boundary. The ordinary-word input bypasses the explicit `is a` safeguard in `_link`. [HTTP](attacks/http_results.json), [dictionary proof](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).
+
+`_whole_name` (`src/query.py:189-195`) erases punctuation and whitespace via `canonical_key` and never checks the stored name's actual token/punctuation boundaries; `_link` correctly enforces boundaries and the single-token abbreviation safeguard (`293-307`). Genuine complete-name controls `CP-358,774` and `Rapamycin, 42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-` have stored full-name matches and correctly retain their intended identities. Their exception need not accept arbitrary new comma lists. Prefixed salt QA stays on the parent drug; the salt counts themselves are accurately stored values.
+
+### M7-S2 — P2: exact stored names are rejected before whole-name lookup
+
+**`(E/Z)-AFATINIB`** uniquely names **CHEMBL1173655**, and `/api/resolve` returns **EXACT**. **`RO 09-1978/000`** uniquely names **CHEMBL1773**, with resolver **ALIAS**. Both `/api/explore` requests return HTTP 200 **not_found**, while the exact IDs draw live nodes. [Actual HTTP, stored-index proof and ID controls](names/alias_http.json).
+
+`src/query.py:189-190` rejects slash/pipe or a listing word before checking whether the input is exactly one stored non-refused name. Thus step (2)'s intended precedence does not cover these valid lone names. The sweep has 20 formerly recognized names with no current Drug, including known M5-S2; it has **zero remaining wrong-ID cases among the 7,511 unique stored keys**. Some lost-alias resolver statuses differ, so the two positively resolved controls above are the strongest should-fix proof. [Complete list](names/names_m5_summary.json). No demo is lost.
+
+### M7-S3 — P2: per-piece full resolution causes new multi-second lock contention
+
+The old cubic all-substring defect remains repaired. The new resolver call for **every list piece**, including identical repeated pieces (`src/query.py:159-164`), introduces a different cost. A same-data, three-sample comparison loads the exact **b7a00f9 query module** and current query module without changing shared rules/data. A second measurement ran after all other audit workers stopped; this isolates audit workloads, not unrelated host activity.
+
+| Exact linker input | Characters | b7a00f9 median | Current median |
+|---|---:|---:|---:|
+| `" ".join(["x"]*250)` | 499 | 0.0219 s | 0.0298 s |
+| `";".join(["x"]*250)` | 499 | 0.0057 s | 0.2537 s |
+| `";".join(["x"]*1000)` | 1,999 | 0.0512 s | 1.3120 s |
+| `";".join(["x"]*2000)` | 3,999 | 0.1779 s | 2.5561 s |
+
+The 2,000-piece stage check spends **2.5653 s** in `_resolver_blocked`, versus **0.2734 s** in `_link`. Measurements are separate calls and need not sum to a median. [Samples/stage timings](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_comparison.json), [reproducer](root_checks/fresh_cost.py).
+
+Actual unchanged CLI HTTP, within existing caps:
+
+- 499-character semicolon text: `/api/explore` **0.3160 s**, `/api/ask` **0.3148 s**, both 200.
+- `POST /api/check` with `{"text":"EGFR","answer":";".join(["x"]*2000)}`: **7.7280 s**, HTTP 200.
+- Ordinary EGFR map dispatched **0.1010 s** after that check: **7.6665 s**, HTTP 200. EGFR before/after: **0.0384 / 0.0412 s**. GET `/` remains responsive.
+- First independent run also reproduced the delay: check **6.0783 s**, queued EGFR **6.0118 s**. No timeout/deadlock inference is needed; these responses completed.
+
+[Isolated HTTP](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_http.json), [first HTTP run](root_checks/fresh_cost_http.json), [owned-server cleanup](root_checks/isolated_fresh_cost_lifecycle.json). The inherited m6 stress script independently records split_2000 at **3.4737 s** median linking / **7.0111 s** `Engine.check`; plain 2,000-token input is far cheaper. [Exact inherited stress](root_checks/cost_stress.json).
+
+The application holds `engine.lock` across linking/checking (`src/app.py:247-263`), and the answer cap is 4,000 characters (`260`). Avoid repeating full resolver work for identical pieces and bound total work across every caller, while preserving the refusal semantics. This is a should-fix cost regression, separate from the originator-leak approval blockers. These timings are local observations, not universal latency guarantees or a claim that the entire algorithm has one asymptotic complexity.
+

These ordinary samples are separate from the adversarial cost measurements. [HTTP demos](root_checks/http_demo_results.json), [HTTP workload](root_checks/http_results.json).

+"""Read-only repository and owned-server checks, with one m7 JSON output."""
+import hashlib
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+import re
+import socket
+import subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+baseline = json.loads((OUT / "root_checks/tracked_initial.json").read_text(encoding="utf8"))
+
+
+def git(*args):
+    result = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
+    return {"exit_code": result.returncode, "stdout": result.stdout.decode("utf8", "replace"),
+            "stderr": result.stderr.decode("utf8", "replace")}
+
+
+changed, missing = [], []
+for name, previous in baseline["hashes"].items():
+    path = ROOT / name
+    if not path.is_file():
+        missing.append(name)
+    elif hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest() != previous:
+        changed.append(name)
+ports = {}
+for port in range(8901, 8906):
+    with socket.socket() as sock:
+        sock.settimeout(.5)
+        ports[str(port)] = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", port)) != 0
+broken = []
+link_count = 0
+for report in [OUT / "REPORT.md", OUT / "names/REPORT.md", OUT / "db_ui/REPORT.md", OUT / "attacks/REPORT.md"]:
+    for href in re.findall(r"\]\(([^)]+)\)", report.read_text(encoding="utf8")):
+        if href.startswith(("http:", "https:", "#")):
+            continue
+        link_count += 1
+        target = href.split("#", 1)[0]
+        if not (report.parent / target).exists() and target != "integrity_final.json":
+            broken.append({"report": str(report.relative_to(OUT)), "href": href})
+head = git("rev-parse", "HEAD")
+diff = git("diff", "--exit-code")
+staged = git("diff", "--cached", "--exit-code")
+status = git("status", "--short")
+result = {"initial_head": baseline["head"], "final_head": head,
+          "head_unchanged": head["stdout"].strip() == baseline["head"],
+          "tracked_files_checked": len(baseline["hashes"]), "changed_tracked_files": changed,
+          "missing_tracked_files": missing, "tracked_diff": diff, "staged_diff": staged,
+          "status": status, "all_owned_ports_closed": ports,
+          "report_local_links_checked": link_count, "broken_report_links": broken,
+          "database_sha256": hashlib.sha256((ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite").read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
+          "main_report_last_line": (OUT / "REPORT.md").read_text(encoding="utf8").rstrip().splitlines()[-1]}
+result["pass"] = bool(result["head_unchanged"] and not changed and not missing and
+                       diff["exit_code"] == 0 and staged["exit_code"] == 0 and
+                       all(ports.values()) and not broken and
+                       result["main_report_last_line"] == "VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED")
+(OUT / "integrity_final.json").write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2), encoding="utf8")
+print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
+raise SystemExit(0 if result["pass"] else 1)

+"""Bounded, same-data cost comparison and owned CLI lock-contention check."""
+import concurrent.futures
+import hashlib
+import json
+import os
+from pathlib import Path
+import socket
+import statistics
+import subprocess
+import sys
+import time
+import types
+import urllib.error
+import urllib.request
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
+PORT = 8902
+BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
+PREFIX = "isolated_" if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == "isolated" else ""
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
+
+
+def dump(name, obj):
+    (OUT / (PREFIX + name)).write_text(json.dumps(obj, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf8")
+
+
+if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == "worker":
+    import query
+    ref, kind, size = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], int(sys.argv[4])
+    mod = query
+    if ref != "HEAD":
+        mod = types.ModuleType("cost_previous")
+        mod.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
+        sys.modules[mod.__name__] = mod
+        code = subprocess.check_output(["git", "show", f"{ref}:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT).decode("utf8")
+        exec(compile(code, mod.__file__, "exec"), mod.__dict__)
+    kg = mod.OncoKG()
+    kg.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
+    value = {"plain": " ", "split": ";", "slash": "/"}[kind].join(["x"] * size)
+    times = []
+    for _ in range(3):
+        start = time.perf_counter()
+        entities = kg.link_entities(value)
+        times.append(time.perf_counter() - start)
+    detail = {}
+    if ref == "HEAD":
+        for name in ["_resolver_blocked", "_link"]:
+            start = time.perf_counter()
+            result = getattr(kg, name)(value)
+            detail[name] = {"seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "result_count": len(result)}
+    kg.conn.close()
+    print(json.dumps({"ref": ref, "kind": kind, "size": size, "characters": len(value),
+                      "times": times, "median": statistics.median(times),
+                      "entities": entities, "stages": detail}))
+    raise SystemExit(0)
+
+
+def post(path, payload, timeout=20):
+    start = time.perf_counter()
+    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
+                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
+    try:
+        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout)
+    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
+        response = error
+    raw = response.read()
+    return {"path": path, "payload": payload, "status": response.status,
+            "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start, "bytes": len(raw), "body": json.loads(raw)}
+
+
+env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
+       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TEMP": str(OUT / "tmp"),
+       "TMP": str(OUT / "tmp"), "TMPDIR": str(OUT / "tmp")}
+rows = []
+for kind, size in [("plain", 250), ("split", 250), ("split", 1000), ("split", 2000)]:
+    for ref in ["b7a00f9", "HEAD"]:
+        try:
+            result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", __file__, "worker", ref, kind, str(size)],
+                                    cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, timeout=35)
+            assert result.returncode == 0, result.stderr.decode("utf8", "replace")
+            row = json.loads(result.stdout)
+        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
+            row = {"ref": ref, "kind": kind, "size": size, "timeout_seconds": 35}
+        rows.append(row)
+        dump("fresh_cost_comparison.json", rows)
+        print(json.dumps(row), flush=True)
+
+db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
+before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+with socket.socket() as sock:
+    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port occupied"
+with (OUT / (PREFIX + "fresh_cost_server.txt")).open("w", encoding="utf8") as log:
+    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
+                            env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
+                            creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
+    try:
+        for _ in range(100):
+            assert proc.poll() is None
+            try:
+                urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
+                break
+            except OSError:
+                time.sleep(.1)
+        else:
+            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
+        baseline = post("/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+        value = ";".join(["x"] * 250)
+        sequential = [post(path, {"text": value}) for path in ["/api/explore", "/api/ask"]]
+        answer = ";".join(["x"] * 2000)
+        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
+            started = time.perf_counter()
+            leader = pool.submit(post, "/api/check", {"text": "EGFR", "answer": answer})
+            time.sleep(.1)
+            dispatched = time.perf_counter() - started
+            follower = pool.submit(post, "/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+            get_start = time.perf_counter()
+            response = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=3)
+            response.read()
+            live_get = {"status": response.status, "seconds": time.perf_counter() - get_start}
+            check, queued = leader.result(), follower.result()
+        after_request = post("/api/explore", {"text": "EGFR"})
+        dump("fresh_cost_http.json", {"baseline": baseline, "sequential": sequential,
+             "check": check, "queued_egfr": queued, "follower_dispatched_seconds": dispatched,
+             "live_get": live_get, "normal_after": after_request})
+        print(json.dumps({"check_seconds": check["seconds"], "queued_egfr_seconds": queued["seconds"],
+                          "normal_after_seconds": after_request["seconds"]}), flush=True)
+    finally:
+        proc.terminate()
+        proc.wait(timeout=10)
+        with socket.socket() as sock:
+            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
+        after = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+        dump("fresh_cost_lifecycle.json", {"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
+             "port_closed": closed, "database_unchanged": before == after,
+             "before": before, "after": after, "exit": proc.returncode})
+        assert closed and before == after

exec
